# Subpolynomial query complexity for well-conditioned log-concave sampling

Coordinate `EN-114-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M1-B-AL-H-0285` | `PM-139.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Subpolynomial-query-complexity-for-well-conditioned-log-concave-sampling-September-26-2026` | date 2026-09-26 | derived from the openai/math preprint Subpolynomial-query-complexity-for-well-conditioned-log-concave-sampling-September-26-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "For every fixed <i>ε</i> &gt; 0, we give a sampling algorithm using at most $`C_\\varepsilon d^\\varepsilon`$ exact first-order queries on every execution for <i>C</i><sup>2</sup> potentials on ℝ<sup><i>d</i></sup> with a known minimizer and Hessian between <i>I</i><sub><i>d</i></sub> and $`2I_d`$. The output has total-variation distance at most 1/10 from the target Gibbs law. Computation between queries is unrestricted. We also prove an $`\\Omega(\\log d)`$ query lower bound for arbitrary randomized adaptive algorithms, determining the optimal dimension exponent to be zero.",
```

```json
      "heading": "References",
      "start_byte": 227689
    }
  ],
  "tokenizer": "o200k_base"
}
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every fixed <i>ε</i> &gt; 0, we give a sampling algorithm using at most $`C_\varepsilon d^\varepsilon`$ exact first-order queries on every execution for <i>C</i><sup>2</sup> potentials on ℝ<sup><i>d</i></sup> with a known minimizer and Hessian between <i>I</i><sub><i>d</i></sub> and $`2I_d`$. The output has total-variation distance at most 1/10 from the target Gibbs law. Computation between queries is unrestricted. We also prove an $`\Omega(\log d)`$ query lower bound for arbitrary randomized adaptive algorithms, determining the optimal dimension exponent to be zero.

- **claim-optimal-dimension-exponent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): In the oracle model above, for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $C_\varepsilon<\infty$ such that $$Q(d)\le C_\varepsilon d^\varepsilon \qquad(d\ge2).$$ There are also absolute constants $c>0$ and $d_0$ such that $$Q(d)\ge c\log d\qquad(d\ge d_0).$$ The lower bound applies to arbitrary randomized adaptive first-order algorithms. In particular, $\gamma_*=0$.
  - Explanation: The sampling query complexity admits every fixed positive dimension exponent, while arbitrary randomized adaptive algorithms still require logarithmically many queries. These bounds determine the optimal exponent without determining the finer growth rate.
- **claim-noisy-near-quadratic-sampling** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-primitive)): Fix $0<e<1$, an integer $K_0\ge2$, and $t>0$ satisfying $$\label{eq:t-choice} 40(K_0+2)t<\frac14.$$ There are constants and a dimension threshold depending only on $e,K_0,t$ with the following property. For every integer $d$ above that threshold, put $\delta=d^{-e}$. One measurable randomized oracle procedure works for every $F\in C^2(\mathbb R^d)$ with $\operatorname{Lip}(\nabla F)\le\delta$, every $x\in\mathbb R^d$, and every $0<r\le1$. Given evaluation access to $g=\nabla F$, it uses at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^{-t}$ evaluations of $g$ on every execution and returns $$X=X_{\mathrm{pre}}+\tfrac12G,$$ where $G\sim N(0,I_d)$ is independent of $X_{\mathrm{pre}}$ and of all queries, and $$\label{eq:primitive-pre-noise} W_2\!\left(\mathcal L(X_{\mathrm{pre}}), \nu_{x,r}*N(0,\tfrac34I_d)\right) \le \operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^{K_0}D(x).$$ Consequently both $$W_2\!\left(\mathcal L(X),\nu_{x,r}*N(0,I_d)\right) \quad\hbox{and}\quad \lVert\mathcal L(X)-\nu_{x,r}*N(0,I_d)\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}$$ are at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^{K_0}D(x)$. All constants in these bounds are uniform in $F,x,r$. The procedure’s scalar parameters, shapes of its computations, and query cap are independent of $x$ and of the primitive. It uses no evaluations of $F$ itself.
  - Explanation: A finite gradient-query procedure approximates the near-quadratic law convolved with an independent Gaussian. Its query cap contains both a polylogarithmic factor and an inverse power of the perturbation parameter; it is not asserted to be polylogarithmic.
- **claim-uniform-convexity-comparisons** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-convex-preliminaries)): Let $H\in C^2(\mathbb R^n)$ satisfy $mI\preceq\nabla^2H\preceq MI$, with $0<m\le M<\infty$. Write $\mu(dx)\propto e^{-H(x)}dx$, and let $z_0$ be its unique mode. Then $$\mathbb E_\mu\|X-z_0\|^2\le n/m,\qquad \operatorname{Var}_\mu h\le m^{-1}\mathbb E_\mu\|\nabla h\|^2.$$ The latter applies, in particular, to $C^1$ functions whose value and gradient have polynomial growth. The diffusion $$dX_t=-\nabla H(X_t)\,dt+\sqrt2\,dB_t$$ has invariant law $\mu$, and synchronous solutions contract in distance by $e^{-mt}$.* *If $\bar H$ is another strongly convex $C^2$ potential with bounded Hessian and law $\eta$, then $$\label{eq:synchronous-comparison} W_2(\mu,\eta) \le \frac1m \left(\mathbb E_\eta\|\nabla H-\nabla\bar H\|^2\right)^{1/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Uniform curvature controls moments around the mode, variance, invariant diffusion dynamics, synchronous contraction, and comparison of Gibbs laws through their gradient difference.
- **claim-conditional-coupling-and-smoothing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-coupling-smoothing)): Let $(\mu_z)$ and $(\eta_z)$ be probability kernels on finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, and let $Z$ have law $\zeta$. Whenever the relevant second moments are finite, $$W_2^2\!\left(\int\mu_z\,d\zeta(z),\int\eta_z\,d\zeta(z)\right) \le\int W_2^2(\mu_z,\eta_z)\,d\zeta(z).$$ For finite families, independent sums satisfy $$W_2\!\left(\mathcal L\Bigl(\sum_i a_iX_i\Bigr), \mathcal L\Bigl(\sum_i a_iY_i\Bigr)\right) \le\sum_i\|a_i\|W_2(\mathcal L(X_i),\mathcal L(Y_i)),$$ where independence is required within each of the two families, not between them. For $s>0$, $$\label{eq:gaussian-smoothing} \lVert\mu*N(0,s^2I)-\eta*N(0,s^2I)\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}} \le s^{-1}W_2(\mu,\eta).$$ Moreover, applying a common probability kernel cannot increase total variation, and $$\left\\|\int\mu_z\,d\zeta(z)-\int\eta_z\,d\zeta(z)\right\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le\int\lVert\mu_z-\eta_z\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\,d\zeta(z).$$
  - Explanation: Conditional couplings control mixture errors, independent sums combine transportation errors, and an independent Gaussian convolution converts transportation control into total variation control.
- **claim-all-split-cumulant-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-appell-cumulants)): Let $S$ be a random vector in $\mathbb R^D$ whose moment-generating function is finite in a neighborhood of zero. Suppose that, for some $\beta\ge0$, $$\operatorname{Var}(P(S))\le\beta\mathbb E\|\nabla P(S)\|^2$$ for every polynomial $P$ on $\mathbb R^D$. For each integer $n\ge2$, define its cumulant tensor by $\kappa_n(S)=\left.\nabla_\theta^n\log\mathbb Ee^{\theta\cdot S}\right\|_{\theta=0}$. Then, for an absolute constant $C$, $$\|\kappa_n(S)\|_{\mathrm{split}} \le C^n n^{Cn}\beta^{n/2}$$ for every nontrivial split. The constant is independent of $D$ and of the mean of $S$.
  - Explanation: A polynomial Poincaré inequality bounds every matrix unfolding of a cumulant tensor independently of the ambient dimension and the mean.
- **claim-gaussian-conditional-jets** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-gaussian-jets)): Under the assumptions above, in particular $l\le1/2$, define for each integer $q\ge1$ $$U_q(\rho,y)=\rho^{-q}\nabla^q M_\rho(y)\qquad(\rho>0).$$ These tensors extend smoothly to $\rho=0$. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for every $0\le\rho<1$, $y\in\mathbb R^d$ and every split, $$\|U_q(\rho,y)\|_{\mathrm{split}} \le C^q q^{Cq}L(\sqrt a)^{-(q-1)}. \label{eq:jets}$$ Here and below the vector-output slot is included among the tensor slots. The constant is uniform in the dimension, the primitive, its center and scale, and the derivative order $q$. Moreover, $DM_\rho$ and $U_1$ are symmetric, and $$\nabla H_\rho(y)=y+r\rho M_\rho(y),\qquad \\|\nabla^2H_\rho\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le C. \label{eq:interpolation-score}$$ For $j\ge1$, every split of $\nabla^j\nabla^2 H_\rho$ is bounded by $$C^{j+1}(j+1)^{C(j+1)}(\sqrt a)^{-j}. \label{eq:score-derivatives}$$
  - Explanation: Gaussian conditioning supplies smooth normalized jets and all-split derivative estimates using only the original potential's stated regularity. The normalization extends to the zero interpolation endpoint.
- **claim-probability-transport** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-transport)): The ordinary differential equation $$\frac{dY_\rho}{d\rho}=-rM_\rho(Y_\rho) \label{eq:transport}$$ transports $\xi_0=N(0,I_d)$ to $\xi_\rho$ for every $\rho<1$. Its velocity is globally $Cl$-Lipschitz in space. On every compact probability-time interval it has a smooth flow in both directions.
  - Explanation: The conditional gradient mean defines a globally Lipschitz velocity whose flow transports the Gaussian starting law to the smoothed marginal at each admissible interpolation time.
- **claim-material-jet-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-material-identities)): The mean and its jets satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal D M&=\nabla^*U_1, \label{eq:material-mean-identity}\\ (\mathcal D U_q)_{i,I} &= (\nabla^*U_{q+1})_{i,I} +2r\rho\sum_{\varnothing\ne S\subseteq I} (U_{\|S\|})_{k,S} (U_{q-\|S\|+1})_{i,k,I\setminus S},\qquad q\ge1. \label{eq:material-jet-identity} \end{aligned}$$ In the second identity $I$ is a labeled list of $q$ derivative slots; subsets refer to positions in that list and the index $k$ is summed. For any smooth tensor field $V$, $$\mathcal D(\nabla^*V) =\nabla^*(\mathcal DV) +r\nabla^*((DM_\rho)V), \label{eq:material-adjoint-identity}$$ where the matrix acts on the slot removed by the divergence. These identities hold also at $\rho=0$ by continuous extension.
  - Explanation: Material differentiation becomes adjoint divergence plus single-slot contractions. The adjoint commutator preserves this structure and the final identities extend to the zero endpoint.
- **claim-single-slot-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-split-contraction)): Let $A$ and $B$ be tensors, and contract one slot of $A$ with one slot of $B$, leaving at least one free slot in each. For any nontrivial split of the resulting free slots there are nontrivial splits of $A$ and $B$ such that $$\|A*B\|_{\mathrm{split}} \le \|A\|_{\mathrm{split}}\|B\|_{\mathrm{split}}.$$
  - Explanation: A single contraction between distinct tensor factors can be oriented as matrix composition for any nontrivial output split, preserving multiplicative split-norm control.
- **claim-tensor-divergence-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-tensor-divergence)): Let $E$ be a tensor expression of weight $k$ in Definition <a href="#def:divergence-expressions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:divergence-expressions">10</a>. There is a constant $C_E\ge1$, depending only on that finite formula, such that for every integer $j\ge0$, every real $2\le p<\infty$, every $0\le\rho<1$, and every nontrivial split, $$\bigl\\|\,\|\nabla^jE\|_{\mathrm{split}}\,\bigr\\|_{L^p(\xi_\rho)} \le C_E^{j+1} \bigl(p+j+\log(d+1)\bigr)^{C_E(j+1)} (\sqrt a)^{-(k+j)}. \label{eq:network}$$ The same $C_E$ works for all $j,p,d,\rho,x,r$. In particular it is not restricted to fixed derivative or moment orders.
  - Explanation: Every admissible finite tensor expression has simultaneous derivative and moment estimates. High trace moments absorb the dimension cost of the sole closing trace.
- **claim-frobenius-divergence-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-divergence-l2)): Let $\mu(dy)\propto e^{-H(y)}dy$, where $H\in C^2(\mathbb R^d)$ has bounded Hessian and $\mu$ has Gaussian tails. Let $V$ be a smooth tensor field with a distinguished divergence slot and with $V,\nabla V\in L^2(\mu)$ in Frobenius norm. Then $$\\|\nabla^*V\\|_{F,2} \le C\bigl(\\|V\\|_{F,2}+\\|\nabla V\\|_{F,2}\bigr), \label{eq:divergence-l2}$$ where $C$ depends only on $\\|\nabla^2H\\|_{\mathrm{op},\infty}$, and is independent of $d$ and the number of remaining tensor slots. For $H=H_\rho$ it is an absolute constant.
  - Explanation: An integration-by-parts estimate bounds adjoint divergence by the Frobenius norms of a field and its gradient, with a constant controlled by the Hessian bound.
- **claim-transported-path-derivatives** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-path-derivatives)): For each fixed integer $m\ge1$, $$\\|\mathcal D^mM_\rho\\|_{L^2(\xi_\rho)} \le\operatorname{polylog}(d)L\sqrt d\,a^{-m}. \label{eq:path-mean}$$ For fixed integers $q\ge1$, $m\ge0$, and fixed real $2\le p<\infty$, every nontrivial split satisfies $$\bigl\\|\,\|\mathcal D^mU_q\|_{\mathrm{split}}\,\bigr\\|_{L^p(\xi_\rho)} \le\operatorname{polylog}(d)L(\sqrt a)^{-(q-1+2m)}. \label{eq:path-jet}$$ The constants hidden in $\operatorname{polylog}(d)$ may depend on $m,q,p$ when these parameters occur, but are uniform in $\rho$, the primitive center, scale, and oracle.
  - Explanation: Iterating the material identities controls jets without repeated dimension losses. A final divergence to a vector introduces the square-root dimension factor.
- **claim-transport-jacobian-derivatives** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transport-derivatives)): For each fixed $k\ge1$, the order-$k$ spatial derivatives of any transport between two times in $[0,b_*]$, after subtracting the identity map, have all nontrivial split norms at most $$C_k lR^{-(k-1)}.$$ For each fixed $m\ge1$ and fixed finite $p\ge1$, uniformly in $0\le\rho\le b_*$, $$\big\\|\\|B_\rho^{(m)}(y)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\big\\|_{L^p(y\sim\xi_*)} \le \operatorname{polylog}(d)\,l(1-\rho^2)^{-m}.$$ The constants and the polylogarithmic factor may depend on the fixed orders and on $p$, but not on the primitive center or scale.
  - Explanation: Spatial differentiation of the flow preserves split estimates, while material jet bounds control time derivatives of the backward transport Jacobian under the endpoint law.
- **claim-centering-kernel** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-centering-kernel)): The field $K$ is smooth and satisfies $$\nabla_{\xi_*}^*K=M_*-u,\qquad \\|K(y)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le CL.$$ For each fixed $j\ge1$, every nontrivial split norm of $\nabla^jK$ is bounded pointwise by $C_jLR^{-j}$.
  - Explanation: Integrating transported jets constructs a smooth matrix field whose row-wise adjoint divergence equals the centered conditional observable. Its operator and derivative bounds support later quadrature.
- **claim-stationary-noisy-mean** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-centering-law)): For $s>0$, let $(Y_0,G_0)$ have law $\xi_*\otimes N(0,I_d)$ and solve $$\label{eq:centering-flow} \dot Y_t=-K(Y_t)^TG_t/s,\qquad \dot G_t=(M_*(Y_t)-u)/s.$$ The solution exists for every finite time, and its law remains $\xi_*\otimes N(0,I_d)$. In particular, $$\label{eq:centering-output} sG_0+\int_0^1M_*(Y_t)\,dt\ \stackrel{\mathrm{law}}= u+sG,\qquad G\sim N(0,I_d).$$
  - Explanation: A complete skew-coupled flow preserves the position and Gaussian momentum product law. Integrating its momentum equation identifies a path integral with a Gaussian centered at the desired mean.
- **claim-stationary-time-derivatives** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stationary-derivatives)): Fix $0<R\le1$. Let a smooth probability density on $\mathbb R^n$, with $n\le Cd$, have Gaussian tails and negative logarithm $H$. Assume its Hessian is bounded and that every positive-order score derivative $\nabla^j\nabla H$ has every one-slot split norm at most $C_jR^{-(j-1)}$, for $j\ge1$. Here a one-slot split isolates one tensor slot as the matrix input and groups all remaining slots as its output. Suppose a stationary autonomous flow has Lie derivative $\mathcal L=\nabla^*A\nabla$, where the adjoint is for this density, $A$ is skew symmetric, and $$\|\nabla^jA\|_{\text{one-slot split}}\le C_j\alpha R^{-j} \quad(j\ge0)$$ for every one-slot split. Let $\phi_0$ be smooth and scalar- or vector-valued, with $$\|\nabla^j\phi_0\|_F\le C_ja_0\sqrt d\,R^{-j} \quad(j\ge1).$$ Assume the fields and all derivatives used have polynomial growth. Then, for each fixed $k\ge1$, the order-$k$ time derivative of $\phi_0$ on the stationary flow has RMS norm at most $$\label{eq:stationary-derivatives} C_k a_0\sqrt d\,(\alpha R^{-2})^k.$$
  - Explanation: Stationarity converts time-derivative errors into fixed-measure estimates. One-slot coefficient bounds multiply Frobenius norms without introducing a further dimension factor at each iteration.
- **claim-global-probability-picard-circuit** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-probability-circuit)): If the input position has marginal law $\xi_{\rho_{\mathrm{in}}}$, then after $N_p$ iterations the maximum, over all state nodes, of the RMS error relative to the exact transport is at most $$\label{eq:probability-picard} C_m\operatorname{polylog}(d)\,l\sqrt d\,h_0^m +(C_ml)^{N_p}CrD.$$ Every computed state is globally Lipschitz in its initial position with constant $C_m$. After subtracting the initial position, its Lipschitz constant is at most $C_ml$.
  - Explanation: Simultaneous Picard layers across all mesh cells control the maximum of nodewise RMS errors while keeping the state map and its correction globally Lipschitz.
- **claim-harmonic-angle-correction-errors** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-angle-errors)): Let $y\sim\xi_*$ and $G\sim N(0,I_d)$ independently. The exact-input angle differentiation in <a href="#eq:kernel-action-derivative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:kernel-action-derivative">[eq:kernel-action-derivative]</a> has RMS truncation error at most $$\label{eq:angle-stencil-error} C_m l\sqrt d\,\psi^{m-1}R^{-2m} =C_ml\sqrt d\,\delta^{2tm-4t}.$$ Before applying the angle differentiation weights, the RMS errors of the computed forward correction values are at most $$\label{eq:kernel-value-error} C_m\operatorname{polylog}(d)\,l\sqrt d\,(h_0^m+\delta^{2tm}) +(C_ml)^{N_p}CrD.$$ Every such approximate correction is globally Lipschitz in $(y,G)$ with constant at most $C_ml$.
  - Explanation: Short stationary harmonic paths permit angle differentiation of forward transport corrections. Keeping the correction rather than the full transported state preserves the small Lipschitz factor.
- **claim-zero-endpoint-kernel-quadrature** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-endpoint-quadrature)): Let $y\sim\xi_*$ and $G\sim N(0,I_d)$ independently. For exact values of $C_\rho(y)G$, the RMS error of this quadrature for $\int_0^{b_*}B'_\rho(y)G\,d\rho/\rho$ is at most $$C_m\operatorname{polylog}(d)\,l\sqrt d\,h_0^{m-2}.$$ The value weights satisfy $$\label{eq:kernel-weight-sum} \sum_i\|q_i\| \le C_mh_0^{-1}(1+\|\log R\|+\|\log h_0\|).$$ These weights are well defined even if the supplied values are approximate: the derivative condition at zero remains exact.
  - Explanation: A Hermite condition enforces the exact zero derivative at the first endpoint, making derivative quadrature against the apparently singular weight well defined even with approximate value inputs.
- **claim-kernel-action-circuit** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-kernel-circuit)): On the stationary product input law, the RMS error of $\widehat v=(\widehat v_Y,\widehat v_G)$ relative to <a href="#eq:centering-flow" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:centering-flow">[eq:centering-flow]</a> is bounded by $\operatorname{polylog}(d)$ times $$\label{eq:kernel-velocity-error} \begin{split} &\frac L\sigma\sqrt d \left[h_0^{m-2}+h_0^{-1}\delta^{-4t} (h_0^m+\delta^{2tm})\right]\\ &\hspace{12mm}+\delta^{-4t-w}(C_ml)^{N_p-1} r\frac L\sigma D. \end{split}$$ It is globally Lipschitz, with constant at most $$\label{eq:velocity-lipschitz} C_m\operatorname{polylog}(d)\,(L/\sigma)\psi^{-1}h_0^{-1}.$$ For every fixed $J\ge1$, its RMS error can be made at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^J D$ with $N_p=O(J+1)$, independently of $w$ and the interpolation orders.
  - Explanation: Scalar mean formulas approximate the kernel action with global Lipschitz control. Algebraic cancellation removes the apparent inverse dependence on the primitive scale.
- **claim-mean-flow-circuit** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-mean-circuit)): For ideal independent anchors $Y_0\sim\xi_*$ and $G_0\sim N(0,I_d)$, and every fixed $J\ge1$, the circuit <a href="#eq:mean-output" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mean-output">[eq:mean-output]</a> can be chosen to differ in RMS by at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\sigma\delta^J D$ from the exact path integral <a href="#eq:centering-output" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:centering-output">[eq:centering-output]</a>. The approximate joint states have RMS errors at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^JD$. Every integration uses $O(J+1)$ Picard layers, with a bound independent of $w$ and all interpolation orders.
  - Explanation: Outer Picard integration and path quadrature approximate the stationary noisy-mean construction on ideal independent anchors, with layer counts independent of mesh and interpolation choices.
- **claim-accuracy-with-fixed-dependency-depth** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-exact-mean-circuits)): Fix $J\ge1$. In all the probability, harmonic, and centering integrations above, one may take at most $$N=\lceil10(J+2)\rceil$$ Picard layers. After any fixed $w\in(0,t)$ is chosen, a common fixed interpolation order satisfying $$\label{eq:interpolation-order-choice} w(m-3)>J+10,\qquad t(m-3)>J+10,\qquad m>4(J+10)$$ suffices for the following conclusions, for all sufficiently large $d$. Probability transport from its exact starting marginal has endpoint RMS error at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^JD$; the same holds after division by an endpoint parameter bounded away from zero. The mean circuit with ideal anchors has RMS error at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\sigma\delta^JD$ relative to <a href="#eq:centering-output" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:centering-output">[eq:centering-output]</a>.* *The number of layers of exact mean dependencies within either circuit is $O((N+1)^2)$, independently of $m$, $w$, and the number of mesh cells. All mesh nodes, interpolation weights, scalar parameters, and iteration counts are deterministic functions of the prescribed parameters and dimension.
  - Explanation: Fixing the accuracy request fixes Picard depth before choosing the mesh exponent and interpolation order. Raising interpolation order improves accuracy without increasing mean dependency depth.
- **claim-affine-numerical-circuit-interface** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-compiler-circuit)): Fix $J\ge1$, the parameters <a href="#eq:numerical-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:numerical-parameters">[eq:numerical-parameters]</a>, and $N=\lceil10(J+2)\rceil$. Choose the fixed interpolation order by <a href="#eq:interpolation-order-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:interpolation-order-choice">[eq:interpolation-order-choice]</a>. For all sufficiently large $d$, the maps <a href="#eq:exact-mean-circuit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exact-mean-circuit">[eq:exact-mean-circuit]</a>–<a href="#eq:exact-sample-circuit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exact-sample-circuit">[eq:exact-sample-circuit]</a> have the following properties.* 1. **Deterministic scalar formulas.* Their exact mean nodes can be ordered as $M_1,\ldots,M_Q$, where $$\label{eq:exact-affine-centers} M_i=u_{E_i,r_i},\qquad E_i=b_i+\sum_{j<i}a_{ij}M_j,\qquad E_f=b_f+\sum_ja_{fj}M_j.$$ All $a_{ij}$ and $a_{fj}$ are deterministic scalars. The whole node list, its scales, and these coefficients can be formed without knowing a mean value or making an oracle query.* 2. **Direct terms and scales.* Each node has $r_i=r\tau_i$ and $$\tau_i=\sqrt{1-\rho_i^2}\ge R/\sqrt5,\qquad b_i=x+r(\ell_i y+m_iG).$$ For $\mathcal C_S$, the $G$ term is absent, $\rho_i\le\rho_e$, and $(\ell_i,m_i)=(\rho_i,0)$. For $\mathcal C_M$, $\rho_i\le b_*$ and the pair is either $(\rho_i,0)$ or $(\rho_i\cos z,\rho_i\sin z)$, with $\|z\|\le C_m\psi$. In particular both coefficients have absolute value at most one. A node for $M_0=u$ has center $x$ and scale $r$. Every mean occurring directly in the final expression for $\mathcal C_M$ is $M_*$ at a computed position and has scale exactly $rR$. The final direct terms are $b_f=\sigma G/2$ and $b_f=y/\rho_e$, respectively.* 3. **Coefficient sums and direct expression size.* There are positive declared bounds $A,A_f$ such that $\sum_{j<i}\|a_{ij}\|\le A$ and $\sum_j\|a_{fj}\|\le A_f$, namely $$\label{eq:coefficient-bounds} \begin{aligned} A_M&=C_1(1+\log d)^{C_1} \bigl[(r/\sigma)\delta^{-4t-c_1w}+r^2\bigr], & A_S&=C_1r^2,\\ A_{f,M}&=C_1, & A_{f,S}&=C_1r, &c_1&=10. \end{aligned}$$ Here $C_1\ge1$ depends only on the fixed parameters and orders. Every direct expression has at most $B_d\delta^{-c_1w}$ terms. Every mean dependency path has length at most $D_0=3N^2+1$; using a larger absolute multiple of $(N+1)^2$ is harmless. This depth bound is independent of $w$, $m$, and the number of mesh cells.* 4. **Errors on the ideal anchor laws.* Under <a href="#eq:numerical-size" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:numerical-size">[eq:numerical-size]</a>, for independent $Y\sim\xi_*$ and $G\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\label{eq:ideal-mean-circuit-error} W_2\bigl(\mathcal L(\mathcal C_M(x,r,\sigma;Y,G)), N(u,\sigma^2I_d/4)\bigr) \le B_d\sigma\delta^JD(x).$$ For $\mathcal C_S$ only $l\le B_d\delta^b$, $b\ge1/2$, is needed. With $Y\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\label{eq:ideal-sample-circuit-error} W_2\bigl(\mathcal L(\mathcal C_S(x,r,\eta;Y)), \nu_{x,r}*N(0,\eta^2I_d/4)\bigr) \le B_d\delta^JD(x).$$ The coefficient and dependency assertions are deterministic, for every choice of anchors. The distributional estimates require the specified ideal anchor laws.
  - Explanation: The finite numerical formulas expose deterministic scalar affine centers, controlled scales, coefficient sums, expression size, and acyclic mean dependencies. Distributional accuracy additionally requires the specified ideal anchor laws.
- **claim-gaussian-center-absorption** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-compiler-absorption)): Let $D_s>0$, and let $\mathsf Q$ satisfy <a href="#eq:mean-shift-warmup" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mean-shift-warmup">[eq:mean-shift-warmup]</a>, with $\\|v\\|_2\le D_s$. Put $$n=\frac r{2D_s},\qquad P=\left(I_m-\frac{vv^T}{4D_s^2}\right)^{1/2}.$$ For a deterministic $x\in\mathbb R^d$, let $Z,G_1,\ldots,G_m$ be independent standard Gaussian vectors in $\mathbb R^d$. Then the output of $\mathsf Q(x+nZ;(P\otimes I_d)G)$ has exactly the law of $\mathsf Q(x;G)$.
  - Explanation: A query-preserving translation and deterministic seed covariance reduction turn a Gaussian-displaced center into exactly the standalone output law at the intended deterministic center.
- **claim-finite-routine-height** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-compiler-height)): Starting at $(b,p)=(1,K_0)$, the rules <a href="#eq:compiler-labels" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:compiler-labels">[eq:compiler-labels]</a> give a finite routine tree of height at most a constant $H=H(K_0,t)$. Every reachable label satisfies $b>1/2$.
  - Explanation: The label transitions force every routine branch to terminate while keeping its size label above the required floor. Routine height is distinct from affine-expression dependency depth.
- **claim-deterministic-query-and-seed-cap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-compiler-count)): One can choose $w>0$, depending only on $K_0,t$ and the fixed Picard counts, and then choose the interpolation orders, so that the entire construction is well defined for all sufficiently large $d$. Every routine reached from a top call satisfies <a href="#eq:shift-budget" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:shift-budget">[eq:shift-budget]</a>. Its total number of seed slots and oracle queries is at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^{-t}$ on every execution.
  - Explanation: Choosing mesh refinement after fixing the routine and expression depths bounds the entire expanded computation. Query caps and seed counts hold pathwise and do not rely on accuracy events.
- **claim-terminal-routine-accuracy** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-compiler-terminal)): In standalone use, a terminal call satisfying <a href="#eq:routine-sizes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:routine-sizes">[eq:routine-sizes]</a> obeys $$\begin{aligned} W_2\bigl(\mathcal L(\mathsf M),N(u,\sigma^2I_d)\bigr) &\le\operatorname{polylog}(d)\sigma\delta^pD(x),\\ W_2\bigl(\mathcal L(\mathsf S_{\rm pre}), \nu_{x,r}*N(0,3\eta^2I_d/4)\bigr) &\le\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^pD(x), \end{aligned}$$ where $\mathsf S_{\rm pre}$ is the output before adding its reserved noise $\eta G_2/2$. The sampling bound also holds after adding that same independent reserve to both laws. The fixed-seed center Lipschitz constant is at most $\lambda$ for $\mathsf M$ and $r\lambda$ for $\mathsf S$.
  - Explanation: Terminal gradient-based formulas approximate the noisy mean and noisy sample targets when the requested error is no stricter than the size label. The same formulas give fixed-seed center stability.
- **claim-descendant-size-propagation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-compiler-sizes)): Starting with a top call satisfying <a href="#eq:routine-sizes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:routine-sizes">[eq:routine-sizes]</a>, every descendant satisfies that condition with its assigned labels, up to a uniform polylogarithmic factor. At each nonterminal circuit, $$\lambda A\le\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^{1/3}. \label{eq:small-coefficients}$$ For nonfinal expression terms within an $\mathsf M$ parent, $\sigma_i\le C\sigma$; within an $\mathsf S$ parent, $r\sigma_i\le C$.
  - Explanation: Declared coefficient bounds and shrinking scales propagate admissible size labels to descendants and make center sensitivity a contraction. Final and nonfinal terms receive different noise allocations.
- **claim-arbitrary-seed-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-compiler-lipschitz)): For every seed array, including correlated or transformed arrays, the compiled $\mathsf M$ output is a function of $x$ with Lipschitz constant at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\lambda$; the normalized $\mathsf S$ output has constant at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)r\lambda$. If the anchors of a nonterminal $\mathsf M$ are treated as separate inputs, its Lipschitz constant in the position anchor is at most $\operatorname{polylog}(d)L$.
  - Explanation: The compiled routines remain Lipschitz in their centers for every fixed seed array, including transformed or correlated arrays. This pathwise estimate is available before any standalone distributional comparison.
- **claim-compiled-routine-accuracy** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-compiler-accuracy)): For every call reached from the top labels, the standalone routines satisfy $$\begin{aligned} W_2(\mathcal L(\mathsf M),N(u,\sigma^2I_d)) &\le\operatorname{polylog}(d)\sigma\delta^pD(x),\\ W_2\bigl(\mathcal L(\mathsf S_{\rm pre}), \nu_{x,r}*N(0,3\eta^2I_d/4)\bigr) &\le\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^pD(x), \end{aligned}$$ where $\mathsf S_{\rm pre}$ denotes the output before its reserved noise. The latter bound also holds after adding the reserved noise to both laws, and $$\\|\mathcal L(\mathsf S)-\nu_{x,r}*N(0,\eta^2I_d)\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \le\operatorname{polylog}(d)\frac{\delta^pD(x)}{\eta}.$$
  - Explanation: Induction first uses fixed-seed stability at inaccurate centers, then absorbs ideal Gaussian center noise, and only afterward invokes the child's standalone law. Reserved independent Gaussian noise supplies total variation control.
- **claim-proximal-kernel-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-proximal-contraction)): The law $\widetilde\pi$ is invariant for $P_h$, and $$W_2(\kappa_{y_1},\kappa_{y_2}) \le\frac{\|y_1-y_2\|}{1+h}.$$ Consequently, for input laws $\mu,\eta$ of finite second moment, $$W_2(\mu P_h,\eta P_h) \le\frac{W_2(\mu,\eta)}{1+h}.$$
  - Explanation: Alternating the ideal Gaussian conditional and Gaussian refresh preserves the noised target. Strong convexity contracts conditional laws and therefore the full transition in quadratic Wasserstein distance.
- **claim-approximate-noised-chain-mixing** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-noised-mixing)): With $K_0>2+1/e$ and $t$ chosen above, start the $\widehat P_h$-chain at zero and run $\lceil Ch^{-1}\log d\rceil+1$ steps. For a sufficiently large fixed $C$, its output has total-variation distance $o(1)$ from $\widetilde\pi$, uniformly over all admissible $V$.
  - Explanation: A prescribed number of proximal calls controls accumulated transportation error. A final smoothed transition converts that control into vanishing total variation error for the noised target.
- **claim-ideal-descent-gradient-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-descent-moments)): If $y\sim\widetilde\pi$ and every conditioning observation preceding level $j$ is ideal, then $$b_j:=\left(\mathbb E\|g_y(x_j)\|^2\right)^{1/2}\le C\sqrt d \qquad(0\le j\le J),$$ with an absolute $C$ for all sufficiently large $d$.
  - Explanation: Under ideal prefixes, shrinking scales make the gradient-moment recurrence summable. These ideal-history moments justify replacing observation kernels in the required order.
- **claim-mode-centered-gaussian-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mode-gaussian)): For any $F\in C^2(\mathbb R^d)$ with $\operatorname{Lip}(\nabla F)\le\lambda$, center $x$, and scale $0<r\le1$ such that $l=\lambda r^2\le1/2$, let $z_0$ be the mode of $\nu_{x,r}$. Then $$\lVert\nu_{x,r}-N(z_0,I_d)\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\le Cl\sqrt d.$$
  - Explanation: A near-quadratic conditional law is close in total variation to the Gaussian centered at its mode. Interpolating the residual potential controls relative entropy through variance estimates.
- **claim-subpolynomial-sampling-upper-bound** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-upper)): For every fixed $\varepsilon>0$, there is $C_\varepsilon<\infty$ such that, for every integer $d\ge2$, a measurable algorithm using the exact value-and-gradient oracle makes at most $C_\varepsilon d^\varepsilon$ queries on every execution and returns a sample within TV distance $1/10$ of $\pi_V$ for every $V$ satisfying <a href="#eq:original-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:original-class">[eq:original-class]</a>. Thus $$Q(d)\le C_\varepsilon d^\varepsilon \qquad(d\ge2).$$
  - Explanation: The noised proximal chain, conditional variance descent, and mode-centered base rule return the unnoised Gibbs target. A capped rejection fallback covers the remaining bounded dimensions.
- **claim-logarithmic-adaptive-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-lower)): There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $d_0$ such that $Q(d)\ge c\log d$ for every $d\ge d_0$. This bound holds even when the inputs are restricted to potentials $V(x)=x^TAx/2$ with $I\preceq A\preceq2I$. The query cap is imposed on every execution, and the algorithm may use arbitrary private randomness independent of its input.
  - Explanation: Even centered Gaussian targets with the stated spectral bounds require logarithmically many exact first-order queries. The lower bound permits arbitrary adaptive query choices and private randomness.
- **claim-deferred-haar-output-representation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-deferred-haar)): Let $\Lambda$ be a fixed real diagonal $d\times d$ matrix, let $O$ be Haar distributed on the orthogonal group, and set $A=O\Lambda O^T$. Consider any measurable randomized algorithm, with private randomness independent of $O$, making at most $q$ adaptive queries to the map $x\mapsto Ax$, where $2q+1<d$, and producing an output $X\in\mathbb R^d$. There is an extension of this experiment carrying independent standard Gaussian vectors $g_1,\ldots,g_{q+1}\in\mathbb R^d$ such that $$\label{eq:krylov-span} O^TX\in\operatorname{span} \{\Lambda^j g_i:1\le i\le q+1,\ 0\le j\le q\} \qquad\text{almost surely}.$$ The joint law of the original variables, including the algorithm’s randomness and $O$, is unchanged. The vectors $g_i$ need not be independent of $O$ or of $A$.
  - Explanation: Deferred revelation of a Haar rotation extends the experiment so that even an unrestricted algorithmic output lies in a Gaussian block-Krylov span. The auxiliary Gaussian vectors need not be independent of the input matrix.
- **claim-uniform-block-span-statistic** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lower-span-statistic)): Suppose $4q+1<2d$, and let $g_1,\ldots,g_{q+1}$ be independent standard Gaussian vectors. Set $$\mathcal K_q=\operatorname{span} \{\Lambda^j g_i:1\le i\le q+1,\ 0\le j\le q\},\qquad \tau_d=\frac{40(q+1)^2}{\sqrt d}.$$ If $\tau_d\le1/2$, then, with probability at least $0.99$, $$\label{eq:lower-uniform-statistic} \|u^TD_pu\|\le2\tau_d\|u\|^2 \qquad\text{for every }u\in\mathcal K_q.$$
  - Explanation: Discrete cosine orthogonality and Gaussian Gram-matrix concentration make the separating quadratic form small simultaneously on the entire block-Krylov span, including transcript-dependent coefficient choices.
- **claim-target-cosine-statistic** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lower-target-statistic)): Let $U\sim N(0,\Lambda^{-1})$, with $\Lambda$ from <a href="#eq:lower-spectrum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lower-spectrum">[eq:lower-spectrum]</a>, and suppose $n=2q+1<d$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \mu_{d,q}:=\mathbb E[U^TD_pU] &=\frac d{\sqrt2}(-\varrho)^n+R_{d,q}, & \|R_{d,q}\|&\le \frac{\sqrt2\,d\,\varrho^{2d-n}}{1-\varrho}, \label{eq:lower-target-mean}\\ \operatorname{Var}(U^TD_pU)&\le2d, & \mathbb E\|U\|^2&\le d,\qquad \operatorname{Var}(\|U\|^2)\le2d. \label{eq:lower-target-variance} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The target Gaussian has a nonvanishing cosine-statistic bias with a controlled Fourier remainder and variance. This contrasts with the uniform block-span estimate.

```json
        "exact_quote": "Let $(\\mu_z)$ and $(\\eta_z)$ be probability kernels on finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, and let $Z$ have law $\\zeta$.       "exact_statement": "Let $(\\mu_z)$ and $(\\eta_z)$ be probability kernels on finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, and let $Z$ have law $\\zeta$. Whenever the relevant second moments are finite, $$W_2^2\\!\\left(\\int\\mu_z\\,d\\zeta(z),\\int\\eta_z\\,d\\zeta(z)\\right)\n       \\le\\int W_2^2(\\mu_z,\\eta_z)\\,d\\zeta(z).$$ For finite families, independent sums satisfy $$W_2\\!\\left(\\mathcal L\\Bigl(\\sum_i a_iX_i\\Bigr),\n             \\mathcal L\\Bigl(\\sum_i a_iY_i\\Bigr)\\right)\n \\le\\sum_i|a_i|W_2(\\mathcal L(X_i),\\mathcal L(Y_i)),$$ where independence is required within each of the two families, not between them. For $s>0$, $$\\label{eq:gaussian-smoothing}\n \\lVert\\mu*N(0,s^2I)-\\eta*N(0,s^2I)\\rVert_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n                       \\le s^{-1}W_2(\\mu,\\eta).$$ Moreover, applying a common probability kernel cannot increase total variation, and $$\\left\\|\\int\\mu_z\\,d\\zeta(z)-\\int\\eta_z\\,d\\zeta(z)\\right\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n       \\le\\int\\lVert\\mu_z-\\eta_z\\rVert_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\,d\\zeta(z).$$",
      "explanation": "Conditional couplings control mixture errors, independent sums combine transportation errors, and an independent Gaussian convolution converts transportation control into total variation control.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-appell-cumulants",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a random vector in $\\mathbb R^D$ whose moment-generating function is finite in a neighborhood of zero. Suppose that, for some $\\beta\\ge0$, $$\\operatorname{Var}(P(S))\\le\\beta\\mathbb E|\\nabla P(S)|^2$$ for every polynomial $P$ on $\\mathbb R^D$. For each integer $n\\ge2$, define its cumulant tensor by $\\kappa_n(S)=\\left.\\nabla_\\theta^n\\log\\mathbb Ee^{\\theta\\cdot S}\\right|_{\\theta=0}$. Then, for an absolute constant $C$, $$|\\kappa_n(S)|_{\\mathrm{split}}\n \\le C^n n^{Cn}\\beta^{n/2}$$ for every nontrivial split. The constant is independent of $D$ and of the mean of $S$.",
        "source_id": "PM-139.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian-jets",
        "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions above, in particular $l\\le1/2$, define for each integer $q\\ge1$ $$U_q(\\rho,y)=\\rho^{-q}\\nabla^q M_\\rho(y)\\qquad(\\rho>0).$$ These tensors extend smoothly to $\\rho=0$. There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for every $0\\le\\rho<1$, $y\\in\\mathbb R^d$ and every split, $$|U_q(\\rho,y)|_{\\mathrm{split}}\n \\le C^q q^{Cq}L(\\sqrt a)^{-(q-1)}.\n \\label{eq:jets}$$ Here and below the vector-output slot is included among the tensor slots. The constant is uniform in the dimension, the primitive, its center and scale, and the derivative order $q$. Moreover, $DM_\\rho$ and $U_1$ are symmetric, and $$\\nabla H_\\rho(y)=y+r\\rho M_\\rho(y),\\qquad\n \\|\\nabla^2H_\\rho\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le C.\n \\label{eq:interpolation-score}$$ For $j\\ge1$, every split of $\\nabla^j\\nabla^2 H_\\rho$ is bounded by $$C^{j+1}(j+1)^{C(j+1)}(\\sqrt a)^{-j}.\n \\label{eq:score-derivatives}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-139.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-material-identities",
        "exact_quote": "The mean and its jets satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal D M&=\\nabla^*U_1,\n \\label{eq:material-mean-identity}\\\\\n (\\mathcal D U_q)_{i,I}\n &= (\\nabla^*U_{q+1})_{i,I}\n   +2r\\rho\\sum_{\\varnothing\\ne S\\subseteq I}\n       (U_{|S|})_{k,S}\n       (U_{q-|S|+1})_{i,k,I\\setminus S},\\qquad q\\ge1.\n \\label{eq:material-jet-identity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In the second identity $I$ is a labeled list of $q$ derivative slots; subsets refer to positions in that list and the index $k$ is summed. For any smooth tensor field $V$, $$\\mathcal D(\\nabla^*V)\n =\\nabla^*(\\mathcal DV)\n   +r\\nabla^*((DM_\\rho)V),\n \\label{eq:material-adjoint-identity}$$ where the matrix acts on the slot removed by the divergence. These identities hold also at $\\rho=0$ by continuous extension.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-divergence-l2",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu(dy)\\propto e^{-H(y)}dy$, where $H\\in C^2(\\mathbb R^d)$ has bounded Hessian and $\\mu$ has Gaussian tails. Then $$\\|\\nabla^*V\\|_{F,2}\n \\le C\\bigl(\\|V\\|_{F,2}+\\|\\nabla V\\|_{F,2}\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:divergence-l2}$$ where $C$ depends only on $\\|\\nabla^2H\\|_{\\mathrm{op},\\infty}$, and is independent of $d$ and the number of remaining tensor slots.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-centering-law",
        "exact_quote": "For $s>0$, let $(Y_0,G_0)$ have law $\\xi_*\\otimes N(0,I_d)$ and solve $$\\label{eq:centering-flow}\n \\dot Y_t=-K(Y_t)^TG_t/s,\\qquad\n \\dot G_t=(M_*(Y_t)-u)/s.$$ The solution exists for every finite time, and its law remains $\\xi_*\\otimes N(0,I_d)$.         "exact_quote": "Fix $0<R\\le1$. Let a smooth probability density on $\\mathbb R^n$, with $n\\le Cd$, have Gaussian tails and negative logarithm $H$. Here a one-slot split isolates one tensor slot as the matrix input and groups all remaining slots as its output. Suppose a stationary autonomous flow has Lie derivative $\\mathcal L=\\nabla^*A\\nabla$, where the adjoint is for this density, $A$ is skew symmetric, and $$|\\nabla^jA|_{\\text{one-slot split}}\\le C_j\\alpha R^{-j}\n \\quad(j\\ge0)$$ for every one-slot split.         "source_id": "PM-139.1"

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel-endpoint-quadrature",
          "exact_quote": "Let $y\\sim\\xi_*$ and $G\\sim N(0,I_d)$ independently.           "source_id": "PM-139.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-kernel-circuit",
        "exact_quote": "On the stationary product input law, the RMS error of $\\widehat v=(\\widehat v_Y,\\widehat v_G)$ relative to <a href=\"#eq:centering-flow\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:centering-flow\">[eq:centering-flow]</a> is bounded by $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)$ times $$\\label{eq:kernel-velocity-error}\n \\begin{split}\n &\\frac L\\sigma\\sqrt d\n \\left[h_0^{m-2}+h_0^{-1}\\delta^{-4t}\n                      (h_0^m+\\delta^{2tm})\\right]\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}+\\delta^{-4t-w}(C_ml)^{N_p-1}\n                           r\\frac L\\sigma D.\n \\end{split}$$ It is globally Lipschitz, with constant at most $$\\label{eq:velocity-lipschitz}\n C_m\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\,(L/\\sigma)\\psi^{-1}h_0^{-1}.$$ For every fixed $J\\ge1$, its RMS error can be made at most $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\delta^J D$ with $N_p=O(J+1)$, independently of $w$ and the interpolation orders.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-exact-mean-circuits",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $J\\ge1$. In all the probability, harmonic, and centering integrations above, one may take at most $$N=\\lceil10(J+2)\\rceil$$ Picard layers. After any fixed $w\\in(0,t)$ is chosen, a common fixed interpolation order satisfying $$\\label{eq:interpolation-order-choice}\n w(m-3)>J+10,\\qquad t(m-3)>J+10,\\qquad m>4(J+10)$$ suffices for the following conclusions, for all sufficiently large $d$. Probability transport from its exact starting marginal has endpoint RMS error at most $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\delta^JD$; the same holds after division by an endpoint parameter bounded away from zero. The mean circuit with ideal anchors has RMS error at most $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\sigma\\delta^JD$ relative to <a href=\"#eq:centering-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:centering-output\">[eq:centering-output]</a>.*\n\n*The number of layers of exact mean dependencies within either circuit is $O((N+1)^2)$, independently of $m$, $w$, and the number of mesh cells. All mesh nodes, interpolation weights, scalar parameters, and iteration counts are deterministic functions of the prescribed parameters and dimension.",

Probability transport from its exact starting marginal has endpoint RMS error at most $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\delta^JD$; the same holds after division by an endpoint parameter bounded away from zero.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compiler-circuit",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:numerical-interface\" label=\"thm:numerical-interface\"></span>Fix $J\\ge1$, the parameters <a href=\"#eq:numerical-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:numerical-parameters\">[eq:numerical-parameters]</a>, and $N=\\lceil10(J+2)\\rceil$. Choose the fixed interpolation order by <a href=\"#eq:interpolation-order-choice\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:interpolation-order-choice\">[eq:interpolation-order-choice]</a>. For all sufficiently large $d$, the maps <a href=\"#eq:exact-mean-circuit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-mean-circuit\">[eq:exact-mean-circuit]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:exact-sample-circuit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-sample-circuit\">[eq:exact-sample-circuit]</a> have the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  **Deterministic scalar formulas.* Their exact mean nodes can be ordered as $M_1,\\ldots,M_Q$, where $$\\label{eq:exact-affine-centers}\n     M_i=u_{E_i,r_i},\\qquad\n     E_i=b_i+\\sum_{j<i}a_{ij}M_j,\\qquad\n     E_f=b_f+\\sum_ja_{fj}M_j.$$ All $a_{ij}$ and $a_{fj}$ are deterministic scalars. The whole node list, its scales, and these coefficients can be formed without knowing a mean value or making an oracle query.*\n\n2.  **Direct terms and scales.* Each node has $r_i=r\\tau_i$ and $$\\tau_i=\\sqrt{1-\\rho_i^2}\\ge R/\\sqrt5,\\qquad\n     b_i=x+r(\\ell_i y+m_iG).$$ For $\\mathcal C_S$, the $G$ term is absent, $\\rho_i\\le\\rho_e$, and $(\\ell_i,m_i)=(\\rho_i,0)$. For $\\mathcal C_M$, $\\rho_i\\le b_*$ and the pair is either $(\\rho_i,0)$ or $(\\rho_i\\cos z,\\rho_i\\sin z)$, with $|z|\\le C_m\\psi$. In particular both coefficients have absolute value at most one. A node for $M_0=u$ has center $x$ and scale $r$. Every mean occurring directly in the final expression for $\\mathcal C_M$ is $M_*$ at a computed position and has scale exactly $rR$. The final direct terms are $b_f=\\sigma G/2$ and $b_f=y/\\rho_e$, respectively.*\n\n\n3.  **Coefficient sums and direct expression size.* There are positive declared bounds $A,A_f$ such that $\\sum_{j<i}|a_{ij}|\\le A$ and $\\sum_j|a_{fj}|\\le A_f$, namely $$\\label{eq:coefficient-bounds}\n     \\begin{aligned}\n     A_M&=C_1(1+\\log d)^{C_1}\n           \\bigl[(r/\\sigma)\\delta^{-4t-c_1w}+r^2\\bigr],\n     & A_S&=C_1r^2,\\\\\n     A_{f,M}&=C_1,\n     & A_{f,S}&=C_1r,\n     &c_1&=10.\n     \\end{aligned}$$ Here $C_1\\ge1$ depends only on the fixed parameters and orders. Every direct expression has at most $B_d\\delta^{-c_1w}$ terms. Every mean dependency path has length at most $D_0=3N^2+1$; using a larger absolute multiple of $(N+1)^2$ is harmless. This depth bound is independent of $w$, $m$, and the number of mesh cells.*\n\n\n\n4.  **Errors on the ideal anchor laws.* Under <a href=\"#eq:numerical-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:numerical-size\">[eq:numerical-size]</a>, for independent $Y\\sim\\xi_*$ and $G\\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-mean-circuit-error}\n     W_2\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\mathcal C_M(x,r,\\sigma;Y,G)),\n                      N(u,\\sigma^2I_d/4)\\bigr)\n           \\le B_d\\sigma\\delta^JD(x).$$ For $\\mathcal C_S$ only $l\\le B_d\\delta^b$, $b\\ge1/2$, is needed. With $Y\\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-sample-circuit-error}\n     W_2\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\mathcal C_S(x,r,\\eta;Y)),\n                        \\nu_{x,r}*N(0,\\eta^2I_d/4)\\bigr)\n           \\le B_d\\delta^JD(x).$$ The coefficient and dependency assertions are deterministic, for every choice of anchors. The distributional estimates require the specified ideal anchor laws.",
        "source_id": "PM-139.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-compiler-circuit",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:numerical-interface\" label=\"thm:numerical-interface\"></span>Fix $J\\ge1$, the parameters <a href=\"#eq:numerical-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:numerical-parameters\">[eq:numerical-parameters]</a>, and $N=\\lceil10(J+2)\\rceil$. Choose the fixed interpolation order by <a href=\"#eq:interpolation-order-choice\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:interpolation-order-choice\">[eq:interpolation-order-choice]</a>. For all sufficiently large $d$, the maps <a href=\"#eq:exact-mean-circuit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-mean-circuit\">[eq:exact-mean-circuit]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:exact-sample-circuit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-sample-circuit\">[eq:exact-sample-circuit]</a> have the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  **Deterministic scalar formulas.* Their exact mean nodes can be ordered as $M_1,\\ldots,M_Q$, where $$\\label{eq:exact-affine-centers}\n     M_i=u_{E_i,r_i},\\qquad\n     E_i=b_i+\\sum_{j<i}a_{ij}M_j,\\qquad\n     E_f=b_f+\\sum_ja_{fj}M_j.$$ All $a_{ij}$ and $a_{fj}$ are deterministic scalars. This depth bound is independent of $w$, $m$, and the number of mesh cells.*\n\n\n\n4.  **Errors on the ideal anchor laws.* Under <a href=\"#eq:numerical-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:numerical-size\">[eq:numerical-size]</a>, for independent $Y\\sim\\xi_*$ and $G\\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-mean-circuit-error}\n     W_2\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\mathcal C_M(x,r,\\sigma;Y,G)),\n                      N(u,\\sigma^2I_d/4)\\bigr)\n           \\le B_d\\sigma\\delta^JD(x).$$ For $\\mathcal C_S$ only $l\\le B_d\\delta^b$, $b\\ge1/2$, is needed. With $Y\\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-sample-circuit-error}\n     W_2\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\mathcal C_S(x,r,\\eta;Y)),\n                        \\nu_{x,r}*N(0,\\eta^2I_d/4)\\bigr)\n           \\le B_d\\delta^JD(x).$$ The coefficient and dependency assertions are deterministic, for every choice of anchors. In particular both coefficients have absolute value at most one. A node for $M_0=u$ has center $x$ and scale $r$. Every mean occurring directly in the final expression for $\\mathcal C_M$ is $M_*$ at a computed position and has scale exactly $rR$. The final direct terms are $b_f=\\sigma G/2$ and $b_f=y/\\rho_e$, respectively.*\n\n\n3.  **Coefficient sums and direct expression size.* There are positive declared bounds $A,A_f$ such that $\\sum_{j<i}|a_{ij}|\\le A$ and $\\sum_j|a_{fj}|\\le A_f$, namely $$\\label{eq:coefficient-bounds}\n     \\begin{aligned}\n     A_M&=C_1(1+\\log d)^{C_1}\n           \\bigl[(r/\\sigma)\\delta^{-4t-c_1w}+r^2\\bigr],\n     & A_S&=C_1r^2,\\\\\n     A_{f,M}&=C_1,\n     & A_{f,S}&=C_1r,\n     &c_1&=10.\n     \\end{aligned}$$ Here $C_1\\ge1$ depends only on the fixed parameters and orders. Every direct expression has at most $B_d\\delta^{-c_1w}$ terms. Every mean dependency path has length at most $D_0=3N^2+1$; using a larger absolute multiple of $(N+1)^2$ is harmless. This depth bound is independent of $w$, $m$, and the number of mesh cells.*\n\n\n\n4.  **Errors on the ideal anchor laws.* Under <a href=\"#eq:numerical-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:numerical-size\">[eq:numerical-size]</a>, for independent $Y\\sim\\xi_*$ and $G\\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-mean-circuit-error}\n     W_2\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\mathcal C_M(x,r,\\sigma;Y,G)),\n                      N(u,\\sigma^2I_d/4)\\bigr)\n           \\le B_d\\sigma\\delta^JD(x).$$ For $\\mathcal C_S$ only $l\\le B_d\\delta^b$, $b\\ge1/2$, is needed. With $Y\\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-sample-circuit-error}\n     W_2\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\mathcal C_S(x,r,\\eta;Y)),\n                        \\nu_{x,r}*N(0,\\eta^2I_d/4)\\bigr)\n           \\le B_d\\delta^JD(x).$$ The coefficient and dependency assertions are deterministic, for every choice of anchors. The distributional estimates require the specified ideal anchor laws.",
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Admissible potential class and oracle model** ([source](#eq:original-class)): For $d\ge2$, let $\mathcal V_d$ consist of the functions $V\in C^2(\mathbb R^d)$ satisfying $$\label{eq:original-class} V(0)=0,\qquad \nabla V(0)=0,\qquad I_d\preceq\nabla^2V(x)\preceq2I_d \quad\text{for every }x\in\mathbb R^d.$$ The target law is $$\pi_V(dx)=Z_V^{-1}e^{-V(x)}\,dx,\qquad Z_V=\int_{\mathbb R^d}e^{-V(x)}\,dx.$$ An exact first-order query at $x$ returns $(V(x),\nabla V(x))$. Queries may be adaptive. Random real variables may be generated, and computation between queries is unrestricted. An algorithm may depend on $d$, but its only information about $V$ is through the oracle responses. All algorithms are measurable.
- **Worst-case query complexity** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $Q(d)$ be the smallest integer $q$ for which an algorithm makes at most $q$ queries on *every* execution and returns $X$ satisfying $\lVert\mathcal L(X)-\pi_V\rVert_{\mathrm{TV}}\le1/10$ for every $V\in\mathcal V_d$. Thus the budget is not merely an expectation.
- **Optimal dimension exponent** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Define the optimal dimension exponent by $$\gamma_*=\inf\{\gamma\ge0:Q(d)\le d^{\gamma+o(1)}\} =\limsup_{d\to\infty}\frac{\log Q(d)}{\log d}.$$
- **Near-quadratic conditional law and mean** ([source](#eq:primitive-law)): For $x\in\mathbb R^d$ and $0<r\le1$, define $$\label{eq:primitive-law} \nu_{x,r}(dz)= \frac{\exp(-\|z\|^2/2-F(x+rz))\,dz} {\int_{\mathbb R^d}\exp(-\|v\|^2/2-F(x+rv))\,dv}, \qquad f(z)=g(x+rz),\qquad u=\mathbb E_{\nu_{x,r}}f.$$
- **Primitive scales** ([source](#eq:primitive-scales)): $$\label{eq:primitive-scales} L=\lambda r,\qquad l=\lambda r^2=rL,\qquad D(x)=\sqrt d+\|g(x)\|.$$
- **Polylogarithmic factors** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Throughout the construction, $\operatorname{polylog}(d)$ denotes a factor bounded by $C(1+\log d)^C$.
- **Gaussian interpolation and conditional gradient mean** ([source](#eq:conditional-tilt)): For independent $Z\sim\nu$ and $N\sim N(0,I_d)$, put $$a=1-\rho^2,\qquad Y=\rho Z+\sqrt a\,N,\qquad \xi_\rho=\mathcal L(Y),\qquad 0\le\rho<1.$$ Write $\xi_\rho(dy)=e^{-H_\rho(y)}\,dy$, incorporating the normalizing constant in $H_\rho$, and define $$M_\rho(y)=\mathbb E[f(Z)\mid Y=y].$$
- **Tensor split norm** ([source](#pm-h-16)): A tensor $T\in(\mathbb R^D)^{\otimes n}$ can be viewed as a matrix by partitioning its $n$ slots into two nonempty sets $A$ and $B$. Contraction on the $A$ slots then defines a linear map $(\mathbb R^D)^{\otimes \|A\|}\to(\mathbb R^D)^{\otimes \|B\|}$, where both tensor spaces carry their Euclidean, or Frobenius, norm. We call the operator norm of this matrix a *split norm*.
- **Cumulant tensor** ([source](#stmt-lem-appell-cumulants)): For each integer $n\ge2$, define its cumulant tensor by $\kappa_n(S)=\left.\nabla_\theta^n\log\mathbb Ee^{\theta\cdot S}\right\|_{\theta=0}$.
- **Normalized conditional jets** ([source](#stmt-prop-gaussian-jets)): Under the assumptions above, in particular $l\le1/2$, define for each integer $q\ge1$ $$U_q(\rho,y)=\rho^{-q}\nabla^q M_\rho(y)\qquad(\rho>0).$$
- **Material derivative** ([source](#sec:divergence)): Along a solution of $Y'_\rho=-rM_\rho(Y_\rho)$, the chain rule gives $$\frac d{d\rho}Q(\rho,Y_\rho) =(\mathcal DQ)(\rho,Y_\rho),\qquad \mathcal D=\partial_\rho-rM_\rho\cdot\nabla.$$
- **Adjoint divergence on a distinguished slot** ([source](#sec:divergence)): If a tensor field $V$ has a distinguished coordinate slot $k$, define its *adjoint divergence on that slot* by $$(\nabla^*V)_I =\sum_{k=1}^d\{-\partial_kV_{I,k} +(\partial_kH_\rho)V_{I,k}\}.$$
- **Tensor expressions and weights** ([source](#stmt-def-divergence-expressions)): A tensor expression is a finite formula built by the following rules. 1. For each integer $q\ge1$, $U_q/L$ is an expression with $q+1$ free slots and weight $q-1$. 2. A permutation of the free slots preserves the weight. 3. Two expressions may be contracted on one chosen slot each, provided each factor retains at least one free slot. The resulting weight is the sum of their weights. 4. An adjoint divergence on a chosen free slot is allowed when at least two free slots remain. It increases the weight by one. The formula specifies its leaves, their orders, and the slots used at each operation. This finite syntactic data is independent of $d,\rho,x,r$ and of the potential. Repeated occurrences are treated as separate factors, even when their values agree.
- **Centering kernel** ([source](#pm-h-27)): Define $$\label{eq:stein-kernel} K(y)^T=\int_0^{b_*}B_\rho(y)U_1(\rho,Y_\rho(y))\,d\rho =\frac1r\int_0^{b_*}B_\rho'(y)\,\frac{d\rho}{\rho}.$$
- **Mean dependency path** ([source](#pm-h-39)): A *mean dependency path* follows a mean node to a mean value used in its center expression, and then repeats.
- **Query-preserving translation** ([source](#eq:mean-shift-warmup)): A vector $v=(v_1,\ldots,v_m)\in\mathbb R^m$ gives a *query-preserving translation* if, for every center, displacement and fixed seed list, $$\label{eq:mean-shift-warmup} \mathsf Q(x+\Delta;z+v\otimes\Delta/r)=\mathsf Q(x;z),$$ and the two computations ask exactly the same physical oracle queries.
- **Reachable and terminal routine calls** ([source](#eq:routine-sizes)): Throughout, a reachable call means a descendant of this call, including the call itself.
- **Gaussian proximal conditional law** ([source](#eq:proximal-conditional)): For $y\in\mathbb R^d$, denote by $\kappa_y$ the probability law with density proportional to $$\label{eq:proximal-conditional} \exp\!\left(-V(x')-\frac{\|x'-y\|^2}{2h}\right).$$
- **Lower-bound spectrum and cosine statistic** ([source](#eq:lower-spectrum)): For the rest of the section define $$\label{eq:lower-spectrum} \theta_k=\frac{(k-1/2)\pi}{d},\qquad \lambda_k=\frac{3+\cos\theta_k}{2},\qquad \Lambda=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_d), \quad 1\le k\le d.$$ All eigenvalues lie strictly between $1$ and $2$. For an integer $q\ge0$ put $$n=2q+1,\qquad p_k=\cos(n\theta_k),\qquad D_p=\operatorname{diag}(p_1,\ldots,p_d).$$ The frequency $n$ exceeds twice the polynomial degree in <a href="#eq:krylov-span" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:krylov-span">[eq:krylov-span]</a>. This makes the quadratic form $u^TD_pu$ small simultaneously for all vectors in that random span.
- **Gaussian block-Krylov span** ([source](#stmt-lem-lower-span-statistic)): Set $$\mathcal K_q=\operatorname{span} \{\Lambda^j g_i:1\le i\le q+1,\ 0\le j\le q\},\qquad \tau_d=\frac{40(q+1)^2}{\sqrt d}.$$

```json
          "exact_quote": "For the rest of the section define $$\\label{eq:lower-spectrum}\n \\theta_k=\\frac{(k-1/2)\\pi}{d},\\qquad\n \\lambda_k=\\frac{3+\\cos\\theta_k}{2},\\qquad\n \\Lambda=\\operatorname{diag}(\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_d),\n \\quad 1\\le k\\le d.$$ All eigenvalues lie strictly between $1$ and $2$. For an integer $q\\ge0$ put $$n=2q+1,\\qquad p_k=\\cos(n\\theta_k),\\qquad\n D_p=\\operatorname{diag}(p_1,\\ldots,p_d).$$ The frequency $n$ exceeds twice the polynomial degree in <a href=\"#eq:krylov-span\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:krylov-span\">[eq:krylov-span]</a>. | q-gaussian-smoothing-error | Total variation after common Gaussian convolution | ≤ | $s^{-1}W_2(\mu,\eta)$ | Positive Gaussian standard deviation and finite relevant transportation distance. |
```
| q-all-split-cumulants | \|\kappa_n(S)\|_{\mathrm{split}} | ≤ | $C^n n^{Cn}\beta^{n/2}$ | Absolute constant independent of ambient dimension and mean. |

| q-picard-layer-cap | Picard layers in each stated integration | ≤ | $\lceil10(J+2)\rceil$ | Layer bound fixed before mesh refinement and interpolation order. |
| q-mean-dependency-depth | Mean dependency path length | ≤ | $3N^2+1$ | Independent of mesh exponent, interpolation order and mesh cell count. |
| q-center-absorption-noise | n | = | $\frac r{2D_s}$ | Positive declared translation budget and primitive scale. |
| q-compiled-query-cap | Total seed slots and oracle queries | ≤ | $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^{-t}$ | Sufficiently large dimension after the prescribed order of parameter choices. |
| q-center-sensitivity-contraction | \lambda A | ≤ | $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^{1/3}$ | Uniform descendant size conditions. |
| q-proximal-conditional-contraction | W_2(\kappa_{y_1},\kappa_{y_2}) | ≤ | $\frac{\|y_1-y_2\|}{1+h}$ | Positive proximal variance. |

| q-upper-construction-query-count | Original first-order query count | ≤ | $\operatorname{polylog}(d)\delta^{-(1+t)}$ | Fixed scalar parameters chosen before oracle responses or random seeds. |
| q-block-span-fluctuation-scale | \tau_d | = | $\frac{40(q+1)^2}{\sqrt d}$ | Frequency restriction and fluctuation condition in the statement. |
| q-target-statistic-variance | \operatorname{Var}(U^TD_pU) | ≤ | $2d$ | Specified cosine frequency and spectrum. |

```json
          "exact_quote": "Let $(\\mu_z)$ and $(\\eta_z)$ be probability kernels on finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, and let $Z$ have law $\\zeta$. Whenever the relevant second moments are finite, $$W_2^2\\!\\left(\\int\\mu_z\\,d\\zeta(z),\\int\\eta_z\\,d\\zeta(z)\\right)\n       \\le\\int W_2^2(\\mu_z,\\eta_z)\\,d\\zeta(z).$$ For finite families, independent sums satisfy $$W_2\\!\\left(\\mathcal L\\Bigl(\\sum_i a_iX_i\\Bigr),\n             \\mathcal L\\Bigl(\\sum_i a_iY_i\\Bigr)\\right)\n \\le\\sum_i|a_i|W_2(\\mathcal L(X_i),\\mathcal L(Y_i)),$$ where independence is required within each of the two families, not between them. For $s>0$, $$\\label{eq:gaussian-smoothing}\n \\lVert\\mu*N(0,s^2I)-\\eta*N(0,s^2I)\\rVert_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n                       \\le s^{-1}W_2(\\mu,\\eta).$$ Moreover, applying a common probability kernel cannot increase total variation, and $$\\left\\|\\int\\mu_z\\,d\\zeta(z)-\\int\\eta_z\\,d\\zeta(z)\\right\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n       \\le\\int\\lVert\\mu_z-\\eta_z\\rVert_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\,d\\zeta(z).$$",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gaussian-jets",
          "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions above, in particular $l\\le1/2$, define for each integer $q\\ge1$ $$U_q(\\rho,y)=\\rho^{-q}\\nabla^q M_\\rho(y)\\qquad(\\rho>0).$$ These tensors extend smoothly to $\\rho=0$. The constant is uniform in the dimension, the primitive, its center and scale, and the derivative order $q$. Moreover, $DM_\\rho$ and $U_1$ are symmetric, and $$\\nabla H_\\rho(y)=y+r\\rho M_\\rho(y),\\qquad\n \\|\\nabla^2H_\\rho\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}\\le C.\n \\label{eq:interpolation-score}$$ For $j\\ge1$, every split of $\\nabla^j\\nabla^2 H_\\rho$ is bounded by $$C^{j+1}(j+1)^{C(j+1)}(\\sqrt a)^{-j}.\n \\label{eq:score-derivatives}$$",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-exact-mean-circuits",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $J\\ge1$. In all the probability, harmonic, and centering integrations above, one may take at most $$N=\\lceil10(J+2)\\rceil$$ Picard layers. After any fixed $w\\in(0,t)$ is chosen, a common fixed interpolation order satisfying $$\\label{eq:interpolation-order-choice}\n w(m-3)>J+10,\\qquad t(m-3)>J+10,\\qquad m>4(J+10)$$ suffices for the following conclusions, for all sufficiently large $d$. Probability transport from its exact starting marginal has endpoint RMS error at most $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\delta^JD$; the same holds after division by an endpoint parameter bounded away from zero. The mean circuit with ideal anchors has RMS error at most $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\sigma\\delta^JD$ relative to <a href=\"#eq:centering-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:centering-output\">[eq:centering-output]</a>.*\n\n*The number of layers of exact mean dependencies within either circuit is $O((N+1)^2)$, independently of $m$, $w$, and the number of mesh cells.           "anchor": "stmt-lem-compiler-circuit",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:numerical-interface\" label=\"thm:numerical-interface\"></span>Fix $J\\ge1$, the parameters <a href=\"#eq:numerical-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:numerical-parameters\">[eq:numerical-parameters]</a>, and $N=\\lceil10(J+2)\\rceil$. Choose the fixed interpolation order by <a href=\"#eq:interpolation-order-choice\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:interpolation-order-choice\">[eq:interpolation-order-choice]</a>. For all sufficiently large $d$, the maps <a href=\"#eq:exact-mean-circuit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-mean-circuit\">[eq:exact-mean-circuit]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:exact-sample-circuit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-sample-circuit\">[eq:exact-sample-circuit]</a> have the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  **Deterministic scalar formulas.* Their exact mean nodes can be ordered as $M_1,\\ldots,M_Q$, where $$\\label{eq:exact-affine-centers}\n     M_i=u_{E_i,r_i},\\qquad\n     E_i=b_i+\\sum_{j<i}a_{ij}M_j,\\qquad\n     E_f=b_f+\\sum_ja_{fj}M_j.$$ All $a_{ij}$ and $a_{fj}$ are deterministic scalars. The whole node list, its scales, and these coefficients can be formed without knowing a mean value or making an oracle query.*\n\n2.  **Direct terms and scales.* Each node has $r_i=r\\tau_i$ and $$\\tau_i=\\sqrt{1-\\rho_i^2}\\ge R/\\sqrt5,\\qquad\n     b_i=x+r(\\ell_i y+m_iG).$$ For $\\mathcal C_S$, the $G$ term is absent, $\\rho_i\\le\\rho_e$, and $(\\ell_i,m_i)=(\\rho_i,0)$. For $\\mathcal C_M$, $\\rho_i\\le b_*$ and the pair is either $(\\rho_i,0)$ or $(\\rho_i\\cos z,\\rho_i\\sin z)$, with $|z|\\le C_m\\psi$. In particular both coefficients have absolute value at most one. A node for $M_0=u$ has center $x$ and scale $r$. Every mean occurring directly in the final expression for $\\mathcal C_M$ is $M_*$ at a computed position and has scale exactly $rR$. The final direct terms are $b_f=\\sigma G/2$ and $b_f=y/\\rho_e$, respectively.*\n\n\n3.  **Coefficient sums and direct expression size.* There are positive declared bounds $A,A_f$ such that $\\sum_{j<i}|a_{ij}|\\le A$ and $\\sum_j|a_{fj}|\\le A_f$, namely $$\\label{eq:coefficient-bounds}\n     \\begin{aligned}\n     A_M&=C_1(1+\\log d)^{C_1}\n           \\bigl[(r/\\sigma)\\delta^{-4t-c_1w}+r^2\\bigr],\n     & A_S&=C_1r^2,\\\\\n     A_{f,M}&=C_1,\n     & A_{f,S}&=C_1r,\n     &c_1&=10.\n     \\end{aligned}$$ Here $C_1\\ge1$ depends only on the fixed parameters and orders. Every direct expression has at most $B_d\\delta^{-c_1w}$ terms. Every mean dependency path has length at most $D_0=3N^2+1$; using a larger absolute multiple of $(N+1)^2$ is harmless. This depth bound is independent of $w$, $m$, and the number of mesh cells.*\n\n\n\n4.  **Errors on the ideal anchor laws.* Under <a href=\"#eq:numerical-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:numerical-size\">[eq:numerical-size]</a>, for independent $Y\\sim\\xi_*$ and $G\\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-mean-circuit-error}\n     W_2\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\mathcal C_M(x,r,\\sigma;Y,G)),\n                      N(u,\\sigma^2I_d/4)\\bigr)\n           \\le B_d\\sigma\\delta^JD(x).$$ For $\\mathcal C_S$ only $l\\le B_d\\delta^b$, $b\\ge1/2$, is needed. With $Y\\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-sample-circuit-error}\n     W_2\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\mathcal C_S(x,r,\\eta;Y)),\n                        \\nu_{x,r}*N(0,\\eta^2I_d/4)\\bigr)\n           \\le B_d\\delta^JD(x).$$ The coefficient and dependency assertions are deterministic, for every choice of anchors. The distributional estimates require the specified ideal anchor laws.",

        }
```

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-convex-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-lem-convex-preliminaries) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-convex-preliminaries): Integration by parts, synchronous diffusion coupling, and regularization. -> Moment, Poincaré, invariance, contraction and transportation comparison estimates.
- **step-coupling-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-lem-coupling-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coupling-smoothing): Conditional product couplings and Gaussian relative entropy. -> Mixture, independent-sum and smoothing comparison rules.
- **step-appell-cumulants** [claim](#stmt-lem-appell-cumulants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-appell-cumulants): Repeated polynomial Poincaré estimates and mixed logarithmic generating functions. -> Dimension-independent cumulant control for every split.
- **step-gaussian-jets** [claim](#stmt-prop-gaussian-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gaussian-jets): Conditional linear-tilt differentiation and all-split cumulant bounds. -> Smooth normalized jets, score identities and derivative estimates.
- **step-probability-transport** [claim](#stmt-prop-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transport): Conditional interpolation velocity and backward transported tests. -> A smooth invertible probability transport on compact interpolation intervals.
- **step-material-identities** [claim](#stmt-lem-material-identities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-material-identities): Differentiate transported expectations and commute material and spatial derivatives. -> Adjoint-divergence and contraction formulas for material derivatives.
- **step-single-contraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-split-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-split-contraction): Orient contracted slots as compatible matrix unfoldings. -> Multiplicative control of every output split norm.
- **step-tensor-divergence** [claim](#stmt-thm-tensor-divergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-tensor-divergence): Structural induction, repeated trace-moment integration by parts, and opening a single closing trace. -> Uniform spatial-derivative and moment estimates for finite tensor expressions.
- **step-frobenius-divergence** [claim](#stmt-lem-divergence-l2) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-divergence-l2): Double integration by parts followed by cutoff approximation. -> A dimension-independent adjoint-divergence estimate.
- **step-path-derivatives** [claim](#stmt-cor-path-derivatives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-path-derivatives): Iterate material identities while reserving the final vector divergence. -> Material jet bounds and a single square-root dimension cost for the mean.
- **step-transport-derivatives** [claim](#stmt-lem-transport-derivatives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transport-derivatives): Differentiate flow equations, attach chain-rule tensors by single contractions, and apply Gronwall estimates. -> Spatial transport estimates and endpoint-law time-derivative estimates for the Jacobian.
- **step-centering-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-centering-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-centering-kernel): Integrate a transported observable against backward transported tests. -> A bounded smooth kernel representing the centered observable by adjoint divergence.
- **step-stationary-centering** [claim](#stmt-prop-centering-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-centering-law): Prove flow completeness and cancellation of the weighted divergence, then integrate momentum. -> Product-law stationarity and an exact noisy-mean path-integral identity.
- **step-stationary-derivatives** [claim](#stmt-lem-stationary-derivatives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stationary-derivatives): Induct on spatial derivatives of iterated skew Lie derivatives. -> Fixed-time RMS derivative bounds without random-path supremum estimates.
- **step-probability-circuit** [claim](#stmt-prop-probability-circuit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-probability-circuit): Global simultaneous Picard iteration with cellwise polynomial quadrature. -> Nodewise RMS error bounds and global Lipschitz correction bounds.
- **step-angle-corrections** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-angle-errors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-angle-errors): Differentiate forward transport corrections along short stationary harmonic trajectories. -> Angle truncation, propagated value-error and global correction-Lipschitz estimates.
- **step-endpoint-quadrature** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-endpoint-quadrature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-endpoint-quadrature): Use a first-cell Hermite interpolant with exact vanishing endpoint derivative. -> Finite derivative quadrature with controlled RMS error and absolute value weights.
- **step-kernel-circuit** [claim](#stmt-prop-kernel-circuit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-kernel-circuit): Combine angle and endpoint quadrature while cancelling the primitive scale algebraically. -> Globally Lipschitz approximate centering velocity with controlled stationary-law error.
- **step-mean-circuit** [claim](#stmt-prop-mean-circuit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-mean-circuit): Outer centering Picard iteration and final observable quadrature. -> Finite noisy-mean formulas with state and output RMS error control.
- **step-fixed-depth-accuracy** [claim](#stmt-prop-exact-mean-circuits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exact-mean-circuits): Choose Picard layers before mesh exponent and interpolation orders, preserving strict error margins. -> Target accuracy with dependency depth independent of interpolation and mesh size.
- **step-affine-interface** [claim](#stmt-lem-compiler-circuit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compiler-circuit): Expand additive states only until earlier mean nodes are reached. -> Deterministic affine centers, coefficient bounds, direct expression sizes and ideal-law errors.
- **step-center-absorption** [claim](#stmt-lem-compiler-absorption) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compiler-absorption): Apply the pathwise translation identity and verify exact Gaussian slot covariance. -> Exact equality with the standalone law at a deterministic center.
- **step-routine-height** [claim](#stmt-lem-compiler-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compiler-height): Count decreasing, positive and neutral label transitions before termination. -> Bounded routine height and admissible size-label floor.
- **step-query-cap** [claim](#stmt-lem-compiler-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compiler-count): Expand uncached expression trees through bounded routine height and construct shifts bottom up. -> Well-defined covariance transforms and deterministic query and seed caps.
- **step-terminal-accuracy** [claim](#stmt-lem-compiler-terminal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compiler-terminal): Compare terminal gradient formulas to the exact mean and mode-centered Gaussian. -> Terminal Wasserstein errors and fixed-seed Lipschitz bounds.
- **step-size-propagation** [claim](#stmt-lem-compiler-sizes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compiler-sizes): Track child scales, output noise allocations and declared coefficient sums. -> All descendant size conditions and small center-sensitivity coefficients.
- **step-fixed-seed-stability** [claim](#stmt-lem-compiler-lipschitz) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compiler-lipschitz): Induct on routine height and affine-expression sensitivity. -> Pathwise center and anchor stability for arbitrary fixed seed arrays.
- **step-compiled-accuracy** [claim](#stmt-prop-compiler-accuracy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-compiler-accuracy): Conditional coupling, fixed-seed stability, Gaussian absorption and standalone induction. -> Standalone mean and pre-reserve sampling accuracy, followed by smoothed total variation accuracy.
- **step-primitive-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-primitive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-compiler-accuracy-2): Invoke the top sampling routine and retain its independent Gaussian reserve. -> Uniform noisy near-quadratic sampling guarantee.
- **step-proximal-contraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-proximal-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-proximal-contraction): Use conditional resampling invariance and synchronous comparison under added Gaussian curvature. -> Invariant noised target and Wasserstein contraction.
- **step-noised-mixing** [claim](#stmt-prop-noised-mixing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-noised-mixing): Sum the perturbed Wasserstein contraction recurrence and add a final smoothing transition. -> Vanishing total variation error for the noised target after a prescribed number of calls.
- **step-descent-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-descent-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-descent-moments): Bound the ideal-history gradient recurrence using summable shrinking scales. -> Uniform RMS gradient control along every ideal prefix.
- **step-mode-gaussian** [claim](#stmt-lem-mode-gaussian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mode-gaussian): Interpolate the mode-centered residual potential and bound relative entropy through Poincaré variance. -> Total variation comparison with the mode-centered Gaussian.
- **step-sampling-upper** [claim](#stmt-thm-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-upper): Combine noised mixing, ordered ideal-prefix replacement, variance descent, Gaussian termination and capped rejection fallback. -> Unnoised target sampling with every fixed positive dimension query exponent.
- **step-deferred-rotation** [claim](#stmt-lem-deferred-haar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-deferred-haar): Reveal partial Haar isometries adaptively and attach Gaussian polar decompositions. -> An unchanged original experiment whose output lies in an auxiliary Gaussian block-Krylov span.
- **step-block-span-statistic** [claim](#stmt-lem-lower-span-statistic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lower-span-statistic): Use half-grid cosine orthogonality and Gaussian Gram-matrix second moments. -> A uniform quadratic-statistic bound over the entire enlarged span.
- **step-target-statistic** [claim](#stmt-lem-lower-target-statistic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lower-target-statistic): Evaluate the reciprocal-spectrum Fourier series and sum independent Gaussian coordinate variances. -> Target bias, controlled Fourier remainder and variance bounds.
- **step-sampling-lower** [claim](#stmt-thm-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lower-target-statistic-2): Separate the joint algorithm and target experiments with a measurable quadratic-statistic event. -> Logarithmic lower bound for arbitrary measurable randomized adaptive algorithms.
- **step-optimal-exponent** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-upper): Combine the independent upper and lower bounds under the common oracle model. -> The optimal dimension exponent is zero, with finer query growth unresolved.

The diffusion $$dX_t=-\\nabla H(X_t)\\,dt+\\sqrt2\\,dB_t$$ has invariant law $\\mu$, and synchronous solutions contract in distance by $e^{-mt}$.*\n\n\n*If $\\bar H$ is another strongly convex $C^2$ potential with bounded Hessian and law $\\eta$, then $$\\label{eq:synchronous-comparison}\n W_2(\\mu,\\eta)\n \\le \\frac1m\n       \\left(\\mathbb E_\\eta|\\nabla H-\\nabla\\bar H|^2\\right)^{1/2}.$$",
