      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cor-path-derivatives",

          "anchor": "stmt-cor-path-derivatives",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed integer $m\\ge1$, $$\\|\\mathcal D^mM_\\rho\\|_{L^2(\\xi_\\rho)}\n \\le\\operatorname{polylog}(d)L\\sqrt d\\,a^{-m}.\n \\label{eq:path-mean}$$ For fixed integers $q\\ge1$, $m\\ge0$, and fixed real $2\\le p<\\infty$, every nontrivial split satisfies $$\\bigl\\|\\,|\\mathcal D^mU_q|_{\\mathrm{split}}\\,\\bigr\\|_{L^p(\\xi_\\rho)}\n \\le\\operatorname{polylog}(d)L(\\sqrt a)^{-(q-1+2m)}.\n \\label{eq:path-jet}$$ The constants hidden in $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)$ may depend on $m,q,p$ when these parameters occur, but are uniform in $\\rho$, the primitive center, scale, and oracle.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stationary-derivatives",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<R\\le1$. Let a smooth probability density on $\\mathbb R^n$, with $n\\le Cd$, have Gaussian tails and negative logarithm $H$. Assume its Hessian is bounded and that every positive-order score derivative $\\nabla^j\\nabla H$ has every one-slot split norm at most $C_jR^{-(j-1)}$, for $j\\ge1$. Here a one-slot split isolates one tensor slot as the matrix input and groups all remaining slots as its output. Suppose a stationary autonomous flow has Lie derivative $\\mathcal L=\\nabla^*A\\nabla$, where the adjoint is for this density, $A$ is skew symmetric, and $$|\\nabla^jA|_{\\text{one-slot split}}\\le C_j\\alpha R^{-j}\n \\quad(j\\ge0)$$ for every one-slot split. Let $\\phi_0$ be smooth and scalar- or vector-valued, with $$|\\nabla^j\\phi_0|_F\\le C_ja_0\\sqrt d\\,R^{-j}\n \\quad(j\\ge1).$$ Assume the fields and all derivatives used have polynomial growth. Then, for each fixed $k\\ge1$, the order-$k$ time derivative of $\\phi_0$ on the stationary flow has RMS norm at most $$\\label{eq:stationary-derivatives}\n C_k a_0\\sqrt d\\,(\\alpha R^{-2})^k.$$",

        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-exact-mean-circuits",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $J\\ge1$. In all the probability, harmonic, and centering integrations above, one may take at most $$N=\\lceil10(J+2)\\rceil$$ Picard layers. After any fixed $w\\in(0,t)$ is chosen, a common fixed interpolation order satisfying $$\\label{eq:interpolation-order-choice}\n w(m-3)>J+10,\\qquad t(m-3)>J+10,\\qquad m>4(J+10)$$ suffices for the following conclusions, for all sufficiently large $d$. Probability transport from its exact starting marginal has endpoint RMS error at most $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\delta^JD$; the same holds after division by an endpoint parameter bounded away from zero. The mean circuit with ideal anchors has RMS error at most $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\sigma\\delta^JD$ relative to <a href=\"#eq:centering-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:centering-output\">[eq:centering-output]</a>.*\n\n*The number of layers of exact mean dependencies within either circuit is $O((N+1)^2)$, independently of $m$, $w$, and the number of mesh cells.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-compiler-circuit",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:numerical-interface\" label=\"thm:numerical-interface\"></span>Fix $J\\ge1$, the parameters <a href=\"#eq:numerical-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:numerical-parameters\">[eq:numerical-parameters]</a>, and $N=\\lceil10(J+2)\\rceil$. Choose the fixed interpolation order by <a href=\"#eq:interpolation-order-choice\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:interpolation-order-choice\">[eq:interpolation-order-choice]</a>. For all sufficiently large $d$, the maps <a href=\"#eq:exact-mean-circuit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-mean-circuit\">[eq:exact-mean-circuit]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:exact-sample-circuit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-sample-circuit\">[eq:exact-sample-circuit]</a> have the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  **Deterministic scalar formulas.* Their exact mean nodes can be ordered as $M_1,\\ldots,M_Q$, where $$\\label{eq:exact-affine-centers}\n     M_i=u_{E_i,r_i},\\qquad\n     E_i=b_i+\\sum_{j<i}a_{ij}M_j,\\qquad\n     E_f=b_f+\\sum_ja_{fj}M_j.$$ All $a_{ij}$ and $a_{fj}$ are deterministic scalars. The whole node list, its scales, and these coefficients can be formed without knowing a mean value or making an oracle query.*\n\n2.  **Direct terms and scales.* Each node has $r_i=r\\tau_i$ and $$\\tau_i=\\sqrt{1-\\rho_i^2}\\ge R/\\sqrt5,\\qquad\n     b_i=x+r(\\ell_i y+m_iG).$$ For $\\mathcal C_S$, the $G$ term is absent, $\\rho_i\\le\\rho_e$, and $(\\ell_i,m_i)=(\\rho_i,0)$. For $\\mathcal C_M$, $\\rho_i\\le b_*$ and the pair is either $(\\rho_i,0)$ or $(\\rho_i\\cos z,\\rho_i\\sin z)$, with $|z|\\le C_m\\psi$. In particular both coefficients have absolute value at most one. A node for $M_0=u$ has center $x$ and scale $r$. Every mean occurring directly in the final expression for $\\mathcal C_M$ is $M_*$ at a computed position and has scale exactly $rR$. The final direct terms are $b_f=\\sigma G/2$ and $b_f=y/\\rho_e$, respectively.*\n\n\n3.  **Coefficient sums and direct expression size.* There are positive declared bounds $A,A_f$ such that $\\sum_{j<i}|a_{ij}|\\le A$ and $\\sum_j|a_{fj}|\\le A_f$, namely $$\\label{eq:coefficient-bounds}\n     \\begin{aligned}\n     A_M&=C_1(1+\\log d)^{C_1}\n           \\bigl[(r/\\sigma)\\delta^{-4t-c_1w}+r^2\\bigr],\n     & A_S&=C_1r^2,\\\\\n     A_{f,M}&=C_1,\n     & A_{f,S}&=C_1r,\n     &c_1&=10.\n     \\end{aligned}$$ Here $C_1\\ge1$ depends only on the fixed parameters and orders. Every direct expression has at most $B_d\\delta^{-c_1w}$ terms. Every mean dependency path has length at most $D_0=3N^2+1$; using a larger absolute multiple of $(N+1)^2$ is harmless. This depth bound is independent of $w$, $m$, and the number of mesh cells.*\n\n\n\n4.  **Errors on the ideal anchor laws.* Under <a href=\"#eq:numerical-size\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:numerical-size\">[eq:numerical-size]</a>, for independent $Y\\sim\\xi_*$ and $G\\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-mean-circuit-error}\n     W_2\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\mathcal C_M(x,r,\\sigma;Y,G)),\n                      N(u,\\sigma^2I_d/4)\\bigr)\n           \\le B_d\\sigma\\delta^JD(x).$$ For $\\mathcal C_S$ only $l\\le B_d\\delta^b$, $b\\ge1/2$, is needed. With $Y\\sim N(0,I_d)$, $$\\label{eq:ideal-sample-circuit-error}\n     W_2\\bigl(\\mathcal L(\\mathcal C_S(x,r,\\eta;Y)),\n                        \\nu_{x,r}*N(0,\\eta^2I_d/4)\\bigr)\n           \\le B_d\\delta^JD(x).$$ The coefficient and dependency assertions are deterministic, for every choice of anchors. The distributional estimates require the specified ideal anchor laws.",

        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-seed-covariance**: The covariance reduction and the compensating Gaussian seed displacement restore identity slot covariance.
- **inv-absorbed-output-law**: A routine satisfying the query-preserving translation identity has the same output law after the prescribed Gaussian center displacement and seed covariance reduction.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-unmodified-seeds**: Displace the center and retain unmodified fresh seeds. -> Translating the noisy center back adds seed variance instead of restoring the standalone seed law.
- **anti-law-without-translation**: Treat covariance cancellation as sufficient for an arbitrary oracle routine. -> The seed transformation does not establish output-law equality without the deterministic query-preserving translation identity.
- **anti-full-sampler-certificate**: Use the finite covariance check as a certificate for the complete Gibbs sampler. -> The check does not implement nested conditional means, establish transport errors, or remove the auxiliary sampling noise.
- **anti-runtime-guarantee**: Interpret the oracle query bound as a practical running-time guarantee. -> The model leaves intermediate real computation unrestricted.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The executable construction is the finite slot-space covariance reduction in the Gaussian absorption lemma. It computes the prescribed positive square root and center-noise scale exactly for rational inputs. It checks covariance restoration, while the output-law conclusion remains conditional on the routine's query-preserving translation identity and independent Gaussian inputs.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with positive rational r, positive rational D, and a nonempty rational vector v.
# PRECONDITIONS: The squared Euclidean norm of v does not exceed D squared.
# OUTPUTS: JSON containing the exact positive square-root matrix P and center-noise scale n.
# TIME: Polynomially many exact symbolic operations in the number of seed slots; bit cost is not bounded here.
# SPACE: A dense slot-space matrix and a vector of exact symbolic values.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic and symbolic square roots using sympy.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception; no approximate covariance is returned.
import json
import sys
from sympy import Rational, sqrt, simplify

def rational(value):
    return Rational(str(value))

def construct(data):
    r = rational(data['r'])
    D = rational(data['D'])
    v = [rational(x) for x in data['v']]
    if not v or r <= 0 or D <= 0:
        raise ValueError('invalid scales or empty vector')
    norm2 = sum(x*x for x in v)
    if norm2 > D*D:
        raise ValueError('translation bound violated')
    if norm2 == 0:
        coefficient = Rational(0)
    else:
        coefficient = (sqrt(1 - norm2/(4*D*D)) - 1)/norm2
    P = [[simplify(int(i == j) + coefficient*v[i]*v[j])
          for j in range(len(v))] for i in range(len(v))]
    n = r/(2*D)
    return {'P': [[str(x) for x in row] for row in P], 'n': str(n)}

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(json.dumps(construct(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))
```

Rational(str(expected['noise']))):\n        return False\n    if not zero(2*D*n - r):\n        return False\n    if any(not zero(x) for x in P - P.T):\n        return False\n    # All principal minors independently establish positive semidefiniteness.\n    for size in range(1, m + 1):\n        for indices in itertools.combinations(range(m), size):\n            minor = simplify(P.extract(indices, indices).det())\n            if minor.is_nonnegative is not True:\n                return False\n    reduced_covariance = eye(m) - eye(m))\n\ntry:\n    result = accept()\nexcept Exception:\n    result = False\nsys.exit(0 if result else 1)\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction establishes an oracle-query guarantee with unrestricted intermediate real computation. It supplies no arithmetic or bit-complexity bound that would justify a production software performance contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The target is a continuous Gibbs law for a globally conditioned potential with a supplied minimizer. The paper does not construct a game-state sampler or establish a simulation-time contract for interactive dynamics. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The analytical circuit interface uses exact conditional means as intermediate nodes. Its recursive implementation is a mathematical oracle procedure, rather than a supplied numerical tool with an error contract for approximate oracle responses. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The paper constructs a measurable sampling algorithm with a query cap on every execution. A contracting noised proximal chain, conditional variance descent, and a mode-centered Gaussian base rule produce the original target law at the prescribed total-variation accuracy. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The implementation explicitly represents affine mean dependencies as an uncached tree and allocates separate ordered seed blocks to each occurrence and its center-supplying subtree. This representation supports the conditional independence and query-preserving shifts used in the accuracy proof. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Simultaneous Picard updates give a dependency structure for the mathematical circuit, but the paper does not analyze processor execution, arithmetic throughput, memory traffic, or an accelerator implementation. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gaussian randomness supports probabilistic sampling and exact covariance identities. No cryptographic unpredictability, adversarial secrecy, or security reduction is established. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sampling theorem may motivate work on suitable continuous posterior laws, but the supplied corpus does not construct a learning procedure or establish applicability to general learned potentials. Its exact oracle and global Hessian hypotheses do not cover stochastic minibatch gradients. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The Gaussian base rule gives a concrete gradient fixed-point iteration for the unique mode of a near-quadratic conditional law. Its contraction controls mode error and supplies the center for the terminal Gaussian approximation. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Disjoint seed blocks and simultaneous circuit layers are local probabilistic and algebraic requirements. The paper does not provide a distributed protocol or establish guarantees under communication delays, node failures, or replicated state. |
| other (Probability transport and Gaussian randomization) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The Gaussian absorption lemma supplies an exact construction that converts an independent Gaussian center displacement into a corrected seed array. For a routine satisfying the pathwise translation identity, deterministic covariance reduction restores precisely the standalone output law. |

Every mean occurring directly in the final expression for $\\mathcal C_M$ is $M_*$ at a computed position and has scale exactly $rR$. <a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-original-target-class** (hypothesis): The original-target sampling guarantee requires the supplied minimizer and the stated global lower and upper Hessian bounds. Blocked use: ['Apply the original-target theorem to a potential with an unknown minimizer.', 'Transfer the theorem to arbitrary convex or nonconvex targets without verifying its curvature hypotheses.']
- **limit-exact-oracle** (formal_scope): Oracle replies are exact. Robustness to approximate or stochastic gradient evaluations is not established by the supplied result. Blocked use: ['Replace exact gradients by minibatch estimates while retaining the sampling guarantee.', 'Treat finite-precision evaluations as exact oracle replies without a separate error analysis.']
- **limit-query-cost-only** (formal_scope): The query bounds do not provide arithmetic, bit-complexity, or practical running-time bounds. Blocked use: ['Interpret a small oracle-query count as a bounded execution-time contract.', 'Infer numerical stability or memory requirements from the query exponent.']
- **limit-every-execution-budget** (formal_scope): The minimax query budget is a cap on every execution rather than merely an expected query count. Blocked use: ['Substitute an expected query bound for the required deterministic cap.', 'Permit unrestricted rejection retries while claiming the stated budget.']
- **limit-fixed-exponent-finer-growth** (unknown): The upper and lower bounds determine the optimal dimension exponent but leave the finer growth of the sampling query complexity unresolved. The construction may depend on the fixed requested exponent. Blocked use: ['Claim a polylogarithmic upper bound for the original sampler.', 'Choose a dimension-dependent exponent and assume the fixed-exponent constants and approximation orders remain uniform.']
- **limit-primitive-parameter-regime** (hypothesis): The auxiliary procedure requires the specified weak perturbation, scale, accuracy parameters, and sufficiently large dimension. Its query cap includes an inverse perturbation power as well as a polylogarithmic factor. Blocked use: ['Apply the primitive to an unrestricted perturbation gradient.', 'Describe the primitive query cap as purely polylogarithmic.', 'Use the asymptotic primitive construction below its parameter-dependent threshold without an additional argument.']
- **limit-primitive-convolution** (approximation): The primitive approximates a Gaussian-convolved law. Its output is not directly a sample from the unnoised target. Blocked use: ['Return the primitive output as a sample from the original Gibbs law.', 'Omit conditional noise removal after the noised proximal chain.']
- **limit-independent-reserved-noise** (hypothesis): The reserved Gaussian must remain independent of the pre-reserve output and all queries. Transportation control is converted to total variation through the prescribed independent smoothing. Blocked use: ["Reuse the reserved Gaussian in the primitive's query computation.", 'Infer unsmoothed total variation control solely from a transportation bound.']
- **limit-tensor-grammar** (formal_scope): The tensor-divergence theorem applies to the specified finite expression grammar. Contractions must retain free slots in both factors, and intermediate adjoint divergences must retain tensor output. Blocked use: ['Apply the all-split theorem to arbitrary tensor networks or self-contractions.', 'Charge the final vector divergence as though it retained the intermediate tensor split bound.']
- **limit-interpolation-endpoint** (hypothesis): Gaussian conditional jets and probability transport are analyzed before the unsmoothed interpolation endpoint. The positive smoothing radius controls the derivative singularities used by quadrature. Blocked use: ['Evaluate endpoint-singular derivative estimates at the unsmoothed endpoint.', 'Remove the smoothing radius while retaining the stated transport and quadrature bounds.']
- **limit-ideal-flow-and-circuit-inputs** (hypothesis): Stationary centering and the numerical circuit error estimates require their specified ideal input laws. Exact conditional means are analytical nodes until the recursive implementation replaces them. Blocked use: ['Assume arbitrary approximate intermediate states have the stationary product law.', 'Treat the centering identity as an already implemented conditional-mean oracle.']
- **limit-nodewise-rms** (formal_scope): Probability-circuit errors control the maximum of nodewise RMS errors. They do not establish an RMS bound for the maximum along a random trajectory. Blocked use: ['Replace fixed-node RMS estimates by an expected path supremum.', 'Infer a simultaneous pathwise error guarantee from the stated numerical error bounds.']
- **limit-kernel-correction-and-zero-endpoint** (hypothesis): The kernel-action construction retains only the forward transport correction and imposes the exact vanishing derivative at the initial endpoint. These conditions support scale cancellation and finite derivative quadrature. Blocked use: ['Replace the forward correction by the full transported state in the kernel formula.', 'Use ordinary first-cell interpolation without the required derivative condition.', 'Approximate a matrix and multiply by arbitrary momentum while assuming the proved global velocity stability.']
- **limit-parameter-choice-order** (hypothesis): Picard counts and dependency bounds are fixed before mesh refinement and interpolation orders. The subsequent parameter choices must preserve the specified size and error conditions. Blocked use: ['Change interpolation or recursion parameters without rechecking the deterministic count and accuracy arguments.', 'Identify expression dependency depth with routine-call height.']
- **limit-absorption-preconditions** (hypothesis): Gaussian absorption requires a pathwise query-preserving translation, its declared shift-vector bound, independent Gaussian inputs, and the prescribed covariance reduction. Blocked use: ['Apply covariance cancellation to an arbitrary routine lacking the translation identity.', 'Displace the center while retaining unmodified fresh seeds.', 'Assume the restored seed array is independent of the displacement Gaussian.']
- **limit-occurrence-independence** (hypothesis): The recursive evaluation uses an uncached expression tree with disjoint seed blocks for separate occurrences and their center-supplying computations. Blocked use: ["Memoize repeated random mean outputs while retaining the proof's conditional independence assertions.", 'Share seed blocks between term occurrences, residual noises, or anchors.']
- **limit-standalone-accuracy-order** (hypothesis): Standalone distributional accuracy assumes independent standard Gaussian seeds. At inaccurate centers the proof first uses fixed-seed stability, then Gaussian absorption, and only afterward invokes a child's standalone law. Blocked use: ['Invoke standalone accuracy directly for transformed seeds at an inaccurate random center.', 'Assume the actual intermediate states have the ideal comparison laws.']
- **limit-noised-chain-stage** (approximation): Approximate proximal mixing approaches the noised target under the stated parameter choices and prescribed chain length. Additional conditional descent is required for the original target. Blocked use: ['Stop after noised mixing and claim original-target accuracy.', 'Infer mixing from contraction without controlling the accumulated primitive errors.']
- **limit-ideal-prefix-descent** (hypothesis): The descent moment bound concerns histories whose preceding conditioning observations are ideal. The error replacement argument must preserve the common ideal prefix. Blocked use: ['Apply the ideal-history moment bound directly to arbitrary actual histories.', 'Reorder observation replacements without verifying the corresponding moment estimates.']
- **limit-mode-gaussian-base** (approximation): The base rule requires a near-quadratic conditional law and a controlled approximation of its unique mode. The Gaussian comparison and the mode-location error are separate obligations. Blocked use: ['Use an arbitrary approximate optimizer as the center while retaining the total variation bound.', 'Apply the terminal Gaussian approximation to an unrestricted nonconvex law.', 'Claim exact conditional sampling from the approximate Gaussian base rule.']
- **limit-bounded-dimension-fallback** (edge_case): The asymptotic construction is completed for smaller dimensions by a capped Gaussian rejection procedure. Overflow returns the prescribed fallback output and contributes to the approximation error. Blocked use: ['Omit the smaller-dimension construction while asserting the guarantee for all admissible dimensions.', 'Remove the rejection cap while retaining an every-execution query budget.']
- **limit-lower-bound-experiment** (counterexample_scope): The lower bound uses admissible centered Gaussian potentials, an every-execution query cap, and private randomness independent of the input. It already obstructs a uniformly successful sampler on the larger admissible class. Blocked use: ['Exclude Gaussian inputs and still cite this proof as a lower bound for the restricted remaining family.', 'Allow input-dependent private randomness while claiming the same deferred-rotation argument.']
- **limit-deferred-gaussian-dependence** (formal_scope): Deferred rotation supplies mutually independent auxiliary Gaussian vectors, but does not assert their independence from the input rotation or matrix. The span statistic must hold uniformly over all coefficient choices. Blocked use: ['Assume the auxiliary Gaussian vectors are independent of the input matrix.', 'Prove a statistic bound only for coefficients fixed independently of the transcript.']
- **limit-finite-covariance-certificate** (formal_scope): A finite exact check of the Gaussian absorption covariance identities can certify the tested algebraic instances. It does not establish the routine's translation identity, Gaussian independence, or the full sampling theorem. Blocked use: ['Promote a covariance-check receipt to a certificate of the complete Gibbs sampler.', 'Infer universal output-law equality for arbitrary routines from agreement on finite covariance examples.']

One measurable randomized oracle procedure works for every $F\\in C^2(\\mathbb R^d)$ with $\\operatorname{Lip}(\\nabla F)\\le\\delta$, every $x\\in\\mathbb R^d$, and every $0<r\\le1$. Given evaluation access to $g=\\nabla F$, it uses at most $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\delta^{-t}$ evaluations of $g$ on every execution and returns $$X=X_{\\mathrm{pre}}+\\tfrac12G,$$ where $G\\sim N(0,I_d)$ is independent of $X_{\\mathrm{pre}}$ and of all queries, and $$\\label{eq:primitive-pre-noise}\n W_2\\!\\left(\\mathcal L(X_{\\mathrm{pre}}),\n       \\nu_{x,r}*N(0,\\tfrac34I_d)\\right)\n       \\le \\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\delta^{K_0}D(x).$$ Consequently both $$W_2\\!\\left(\\mathcal L(X),\\nu_{x,r}*N(0,I_d)\\right)\n \\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad\n \\lVert\\mathcal L(X)-\\nu_{x,r}*N(0,I_d)\\rVert_{\\mathrm{TV}}$$ are at most $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)\\delta^{K_0}D(x)$. All constants in these bounds are uniform in $F,x,r$. The procedure’s scalar parameters, shapes of its computations, and query cap are independent of $x$ and of the primitive. It uses no evaluations of $F$ itself.",
          "source_id": "PM-139.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-coupling-smoothing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(\\mu_z)$ and $(\\eta_z)$ be probability kernels on finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, and let $Z$ have law $\\zeta$. Whenever the relevant second moments are finite, $$W_2^2\\!\\left(\\int\\mu_z\\,d\\zeta(z),\\int\\eta_z\\,d\\zeta(z)\\right)\n       \\le\\int W_2^2(\\mu_z,\\eta_z)\\,d\\zeta(z).$$ For finite families, independent sums satisfy $$W_2\\!\\left(\\mathcal L\\Bigl(\\sum_i a_iX_i\\Bigr),\n             \\mathcal L\\Bigl(\\sum_i a_iY_i\\Bigr)\\right)\n \\le\\sum_i|a_i|W_2(\\mathcal L(X_i),\\mathcal L(Y_i)),$$ where independence is required within each of the two families, not between them. For $s>0$, $$\\label{eq:gaussian-smoothing}\n \\lVert\\mu*N(0,s^2I)-\\eta*N(0,s^2I)\\rVert_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n                       \\le s^{-1}W_2(\\mu,\\eta).$$ Moreover, applying a common probability kernel cannot increase total variation, and $$\\left\\|\\int\\mu_z\\,d\\zeta(z)-\\int\\eta_z\\,d\\zeta(z)\\right\\|_{\\mathrm{TV}}\n       \\le\\int\\lVert\\mu_z-\\eta_z\\rVert_{\\mathrm{TV}}\\,d\\zeta(z).$$",

          "anchor": "stmt-def-divergence-expressions",
          "exact_quote": "A tensor expression is a finite formula built by the following rules.\n\n1.  For each integer $q\\ge1$, $U_q/L$ is an expression with $q+1$ free slots and weight $q-1$.\n\n2.  A permutation of the free slots preserves the weight.\n\n3.  Two expressions may be contracted on one chosen slot each, provided each factor retains at least one free slot. The resulting weight is the sum of their weights.\n\n4.  An adjoint divergence on a chosen free slot is allowed when at least two free slots remain. It increases the weight by one.\n\nThe formula specifies its leaves, their orders, and the slots used at each operation. This finite syntactic data is independent of $d,\\rho,x,r$ and of the potential. Repeated occurrences are treated as separate factors, even when their values agree.",
          "source_id": "PM-139.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-path-derivatives",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed integer $m\\ge1$, $$\\|\\mathcal D^mM_\\rho\\|_{L^2(\\xi_\\rho)}\n \\le\\operatorname{polylog}(d)L\\sqrt d\\,a^{-m}.\n \\label{eq:path-mean}$$ For fixed integers $q\\ge1$, $m\\ge0$, and fixed real $2\\le p<\\infty$, every nontrivial split satisfies $$\\bigl\\|\\,|\\mathcal D^mU_q|_{\\mathrm{split}}\\,\\bigr\\|_{L^p(\\xi_\\rho)}\n \\le\\operatorname{polylog}(d)L(\\sqrt a)^{-(q-1+2m)}.\n \\label{eq:path-jet}$$ The constants hidden in $\\operatorname{polylog}(d)$ may depend on $m,q,p$ when these parameters occur, but are uniform in $\\rho$, the primitive center, scale, and oracle.",
          "source_id": "PM-139.1"
        }

There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for every $0\\le\\rho<1$, $y\\in\\mathbb R^d$ and every split, $$|U_q(\\rho,y)|_{\\mathrm{split}}\n \\le C^q q^{Cq}L(\\sqrt a)^{-(q-1)}.\n \\label{eq:jets}$$ Here and below the vector-output slot is included among the tensor slots. The constant is uniform in the dimension, the primitive, its center and scale, and the derivative order $q$.           "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $d_0$ such that $Q(d)\\ge c\\log d$ for every $d\\ge d_0$. This bound holds even when the inputs are restricted to potentials $V(x)=x^TAx/2$ with $I\\preceq A\\preceq2I$. The query cap is imposed on every execution, and the algorithm may use arbitrary private randomness independent of its input.",
          "source_id": "PM-139.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-deferred-haar",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Lambda$ be a fixed real diagonal $d\\times d$ matrix, let $O$ be Haar distributed on the orthogonal group, and set $A=O\\Lambda O^T$. Consider any measurable randomized algorithm, with private randomness independent of $O$, making at most $q$ adaptive queries to the map $x\\mapsto Ax$, where $2q+1<d$, and producing an output $X\\in\\mathbb R^d$. There is an extension of this experiment carrying independent standard Gaussian vectors $g_1,\\ldots,g_{q+1}\\in\\mathbb R^d$ such that $$\\label{eq:krylov-span}\n O^TX\\in\\operatorname{span}\n \\{\\Lambda^j g_i:1\\le i\\le q+1,\\ 0\\le j\\le q\\}\n \\qquad\\text{almost surely}.$$ The joint law of the original variables, including the algorithm’s randomness and $O$, is unchanged. The vectors $g_i$ need not be independent of $O$ or of $A$.",
          "source_id": "PM-139.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-lower-bound-experiment",

      "statement": "The lower bound uses admissible centered Gaussian potentials, an every-execution query cap, and private randomness independent of the input. It already obstructs a uniformly successful sampler on the larger admissible class.",

    },

          "source_id": "PM-139.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-lower-span-statistic",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $4q+1<2d$, and let $g_1,\\ldots,g_{q+1}$ be independent standard Gaussian vectors. Set $$\\mathcal K_q=\\operatorname{span}\n \\{\\Lambda^j g_i:1\\le i\\le q+1,\\ 0\\le j\\le q\\},\\qquad\n \\tau_d=\\frac{40(q+1)^2}{\\sqrt d}.$$ If $\\tau_d\\le1/2$, then, with probability at least $0.99$, $$\\label{eq:lower-uniform-statistic}\n |u^TD_pu|\\le2\\tau_d|u|^2\n \\qquad\\text{for every }u\\in\\mathcal K_q.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-139.1"

Put $$n=\\frac r{2D_s},\\qquad\n P=\\left(I_m-\\frac{vv^T}{4D_s^2}\\right)^{1/2}.$$ For a deterministic $x\\in\\mathbb R^d$, let $Z,G_1,\\ldots,G_m$ be independent standard Gaussian vectors in $\\mathbb R^d$. <a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 139 (`lean/docs/139.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Subpolynomial query complexity for well-conditioned log-concave sampling](../../preprints/Subpolynomial-query-complexity-for-well-conditioned-log-concave-sampling-September-26-2026/article.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result determines the dimension exponent of exact value-and-gradient query complexity for well-conditioned log-concave sampling. For potentials with $V(0)=0$, $\nabla V(0)=0$, and $I\le\nabla^2V\le2I$, the least worst-case query budget achieving total-variation error at most $1/10$ is at most $C_\varepsilon d^\varepsilon$ for every $\varepsilon>0$, and at least $c\log d$ eventually. Hence the infimal polynomial exponent is zero. Algorithms may be measurable, randomized, and adaptive; arithmetic and bit costs are not bounded.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open MeasureTheory Filter
open scoped ENNReal NNReal Topology

namespace LogConcaveSampling

abbrev Point (d : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin d)
abbrev Reply (d : ℕ) := ℝ × Point d
abbrev Transcript (d q : ℕ) := Fin q → Reply d

/-- Normalized twice continuously differentiable potentials with Hessian between the identity and twice the identity. -/
structure Admissible {d : ℕ} (V : Point d → ℝ) : Prop where
  smooth : ContDiff ℝ 2 V
  value_zero : V 0 = 0
  gradient_zero : gradient V 0 = 0
  hessian_bounds : ∀ x v : Point d,
    ‖v‖ ^ 2 ≤ inner ℝ v (fderiv ℝ (gradient V) x v) ∧
    inner ℝ v (fderiv ℝ (gradient V) x v) ≤ 2 * ‖v‖ ^ 2

/-- The unnormalized Gibbs density relative to Euclidean volume. -/
def gibbsDensity {d : ℕ} (V : Point d → ℝ) (x : Point d) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ENNReal.ofReal (Real.exp (-V x))

def partition {d : ℕ} (V : Point d → ℝ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ∫⁻ x, gibbsDensity V x ∂volume

/-- The Gibbs probability law, normalized by its partition function. -/
def gibbs {d : ℕ} (V : Point d → ℝ) : Measure (Point d) :=
  (partition V)⁻¹ • volume.withDensity (gibbsDensity V)

def TVAtMost {E : Type*} [MeasurableSpace E]
    (μ ν : Measure E) (ε : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ s : Set E, MeasurableSet s → |μ.real s - ν.real s| ≤ ε

def firstOrderReply {d : ℕ} (V : Point d → ℝ) (x : Point d) : Reply d :=
  (V x, gradient V x)

/-- Measurable adaptive queries and output, parametrized by private randomness. -/
structure OracleAlgorithm (Ω : Type*) [MeasurableSpace Ω] (d q : ℕ) where
  query : Fin q → Ω × Transcript d q → Point d
  query_measurable : ∀ i, Measurable (query i)
  output : Ω × Transcript d q → Point d
  output_measurable : Measurable output

def OracleAlgorithm.history {Ω : Type*} [MeasurableSpace Ω] {d q : ℕ}
    (A : OracleAlgorithm Ω d q) (V : Point d → ℝ) (ω : Ω) : ℕ → Transcript d q
  | 0 => 0
  | n + 1 =>
      let h := A.history V ω n
      if hn : n < q then
        Function.update h ⟨n, hn⟩ (firstOrderReply V (A.query ⟨n, hn⟩ (ω, h)))
      else h

def OracleAlgorithm.run {Ω : Type*} [MeasurableSpace Ω] {d q : ℕ}
    (A : OracleAlgorithm Ω d q) (V : Point d → ℝ) (ω : Ω) : Point d :=
  A.output (ω, A.history V ω q)

/-- A uniform sampler with a deterministic query budget on every execution and total-variation error at most one tenth. -/
def CanSample (d q : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ (Ω : Type) (_ : MeasurableSpace Ω) (μ : Measure Ω)
    (_ : IsProbabilityMeasure μ) (A : OracleAlgorithm Ω d q),
    ∀ V : Point d → ℝ, Admissible V →
      Measurable (A.run V) ∧ TVAtMost (μ.map (A.run V)) (gibbs V) (1 / 10)

/-- The smallest feasible integer query budget, or infinity when no budget is feasible. -/
def queryComplexity (d : ℕ) : ℕ∞ :=
  sInf ((fun q : ℕ => (q : ℕ∞)) '' {q | CanSample d q})

/-- The quantified dimension-exponent upper bound with every positive exponent slack. -/
def ExponentBound (γ : ℝ) : Prop :=
  0 ≤ γ ∧ ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ d₀ : ℕ, ∀ d : ℕ, d₀ ≤ d →
    (queryComplexity d).toENNReal ≤ ENNReal.ofReal ((d : ℝ) ^ (γ + ε))

def gammaStar : ℝ := sInf {γ : ℝ | ExponentBound γ}

theorem exact_source_main :
    (∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ d : ℕ, 2 ≤ d →
      (queryComplexity d).toENNReal ≤ ENNReal.ofReal (C * (d : ℝ) ^ ε)) ∧
    (∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ ∃ d₀ : ℕ, 2 ≤ d₀ ∧ ∀ d : ℕ, d₀ ≤ d →
      ENNReal.ofReal (c * Real.log (d : ℝ)) ≤ (queryComplexity d).toENNReal) ∧
    gammaStar = 0 := by
  sorry

end LogConcaveSampling

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every fixed <i>ε</i> &gt; 0, we give a sampling algorithm using at most $`C_\varepsilon d^\varepsilon`$ exact first-order queries on every execution for <i>C</i><sup>2</sup> potentials on ℝ<sup><i>d</i></sup> with a known minimizer and Hessian between <i>I</i><sub><i>d</i></sub> and $`2I_d`$. The output has total-variation distance at most 1/10 from the target Gibbs law. Computation between queries is unrestricted. We also prove an $`\Omega(\log d)`$ query lower bound for arbitrary randomized adaptive algorithms, determining the optimal dimension exponent to be zero.
